          <p>(4) for corrective actions to address incidents involving agricultural chemicals, including related administrative, enforcement, and cost recovery actions pursuant to chapter 18D; and</p>
          <p>(5) together with any amount approved for transfer to the agency from the petroleum tank fund by the commissioner of management and budget, to take actions related to releases of petroleum as provided under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115C.08">115C.08</a>.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner of management and budget shall allocate the amounts available in any biennium to the agency, and the commissioners of agriculture and natural resources for the purposes provided in this subdivision based upon work plans submitted by the agency and the commissioners of agriculture and natural resources, and may adjust those allocations upon submittal of revised work plans. Copies of the work plans shall be submitted to the chairs of the senate and house of representatives committees having jurisdiction over environment and environment finance.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.155.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Revenues.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">The following revenues shall be deposited in the general portion of the remediation fund:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) response costs related to releases of hazardous substances, or pollutants or contaminants, recovered under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.17">115B.17</a>, subdivision 6; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.443">115B.443</a>; or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.444">115B.444</a> or any other law;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) money paid to the agency or the Agriculture Department by voluntary parties who have received technical or other assistance under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.17#stat.115B.17.14">115B.17, subdivision 14</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.175">115B.175 </a>to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.179">115B.179</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115C.03#stat.115C.03.9">115C.03, subdivision 9</a>;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) money received in the form of gifts, grants, reimbursement, or appropriation from any source for any of the purposes provided in subdivision 2, except federal grants; and</p>
          <p class="in">(4) interest accrued on the fund.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.155.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Dry cleaner environmental response and reimbursement account.</span></h2>
          <p>The dry cleaner environmental response and reimbursement account is as described in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.47">115B.47</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.51">115B.51</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.155.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Metropolitan landfill contingency action trust account.</span></h2>
          <p>The metropolitan landfill contingency action trust account is as described in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/473.845">473.845</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.155.5a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5a.<span class="headnote">Water quality and sustainability account.</span></h2>
          <p>The water quality and sustainability account is as described in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.52">115B.52</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.155.5b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5b.<span class="headnote">Natural resources damages account.</span></h2>
          <p>The natural resources damages account is as described in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.172">115B.172</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.155.5c">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5c.<span class="headnote">Closed landfill solar redevelopment and reuse account.</span></h2>
          <p>The closed landfill solar redevelopment and reuse account is established and managed as provided under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.431">115B.431</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.155.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Transfers to fund.</span></h2>
          <p>The remediation fund shall also be supported by transfers as may be authorized by the legislature from time to time from the environmental fund.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.155.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/128/#laws.2.39.0">2003 c 128 art 2 s 39</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.2.161.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 2 s 161</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/179/#laws.0.35.0">2008 c 179 s 35</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/101/#laws.2.109.0">2009 c 101 art 2 s 109</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/382/#laws.0.19.0">2010 c 382 s 19</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2018/0/204/#laws.0.3.0">2018 c 204 s 3</a>,4; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/4/#laws.3.100.0">1Sp2019 c 4 art 3 s 100</a>-102; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2020/0/83/#laws.1.19.0">2020 c 83 art 1 s 19</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/4/#laws.8.4.0">1Sp2021 c 4 art 8 s 4</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116.156"><b>116.156</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/93/#laws.1.47.0">2009 c 93 art 1 s 47</a>]</div>
      <h1 class="fgh">WATER POLLUTION CONTROL PROGRAM</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116.16">
        <h1 class="shn">116.16 MINNESOTA STATE WATER POLLUTION CONTROL PROGRAM.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.16.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Purpose.</span></h2>
          <p>A Minnesota state water pollution control program is created to provide money to be granted or loaned to agencies and subdivisions of the state for the acquisition and betterment of public land, buildings, and improvements of a capital nature needed for the prevention, control, and abatement of water pollution in accordance with the long-range state policy, plan, and program established in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115.41">115.41</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115.63">115.63</a>, and in accordance with standards adopted pursuant to law by the Minnesota Pollution Control Agency. It is determined that state financial assistance for the construction of water pollution prevention and abatement facilities for municipal disposal systems and combined sewer overflow is a public purpose and a proper function of state government, in that the state is trustee of the waters of the state and such financial assistance is necessary to protect the purity of state waters, and to protect the public health of the citizens of the state, which is endangered whenever pollution enters state waters at one point and flows to other points in the state.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.16.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>In this section and sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.17">116.17</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.18">116.18</a>:</p>
          <p>(1) "agency" means the Minnesota Pollution Control Agency created by this chapter;</p>
          <p>(2) "municipality" means any county, city, town, the metropolitan council, or an Indian tribe or an authorized Indian tribal organization, and any other governmental subdivision of the state responsible by law for the prevention, control, and abatement of water pollution in any area of the state;</p>
          <p>(3) "water pollution control program" means the Minnesota state water pollution control program created by subdivision 1;</p>
          <p>(4) "bond account" means the Minnesota state water pollution control bond account created in the state bond fund by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.17#stat.116.17.4">116.17, subdivision 4</a>;</p>
          <p>(5) terms defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115.01">115.01</a> have the meanings therein given them;</p>
          <p>(6) the eligible cost of any municipal project, except as otherwise provided in clause (7), includes (i) preliminary planning to determine the economic, engineering, and environmental feasibility of the project; (ii) engineering, architectural, legal, fiscal, economic, sociological, project administrative costs of the agency and the municipality, and other investigations and studies; (iii) surveys, designs, plans, working drawings, specifications, procedures, and other actions necessary to the planning, design, and construction of the project; (iv) erection, building, acquisition, alteration, remodeling, improvement, and extension of disposal systems; (v) inspection and supervision of construction; and (vi) all other expenses of the kinds enumerated in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/475.65">475.65</a>;</p>
          <p>(7) for state grants under the state independent grants program, the eligible cost includes the acquisition of land for stabilization ponds, the construction of collector sewers for totally unsewered statutory and home rule charter cities and towns described under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/368.01#stat.368.01.1">368.01, subdivision 1</a> or 1a, that are in existence on January 1, 1985, and the provision of reserve capacity sufficient to serve the reasonable needs of the municipality for 20 years in the case of treatment works and 40 years in the case of sewer systems. For state grants under the state independent grants program, the eligible cost does not include the provision of service to seasonal homes, or cost increases from contingencies that exceed three percent of as-bid costs or cost increases from unanticipated site conditions that exceed an additional two percent of as-bid costs;</p>
          <p>(8) "authority" means the Minnesota Public Facilities Authority established in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/446A.03">446A.03</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.16.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Receipts.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of management and budget shall deposit in the state treasury and credit to a separate account in the bond proceeds fund as received all proceeds of Minnesota water pollution control bonds, except accrued interest and premiums received upon the sale thereof. All money granted to the state for such purposes by the federal government or any agency thereof must be credited to a separate account in the federal fund. All such receipts are annually appropriated for the permanent construction and improvement purposes of the water pollution control program, and shall be and remain available for expenditure in accordance with this section and federal law until the purposes for which such appropriations were made have been accomplished or abandoned.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.16.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Disbursements.</span></h2>
          <p>Disbursements for the water pollution control program shall be made by the commissioner of management and budget at the times and in the amounts requested by the agency or the Minnesota Public Facilities Authority in accordance with the applicable state and federal law governing such disbursements; except that no appropriation or loan of state funds for any project shall be disbursed to any municipality until and unless the agency has by resolution determined the total estimated cost of the project, and ascertained that financing of the project is assured by:</p>
          <p>(1) a grant to the municipality by an agency of the federal government within the amount of funds then appropriated to that agency and allocated by it to projects within the state; or</p>
          <p>(2) a grant of funds appropriated by state law; or</p>
          <p>(3) a loan authorized by state law; or</p>
          <p>(4) the appropriation of proceeds of bonds or other funds of the municipality to a fund for the construction of the project; or</p>
          <p>(5) any or all of the means referred to in clauses (1) to (4); and</p>
          <p>(6) an irrevocable undertaking, by resolution of the governing body of the municipality, to use all funds so made available exclusively for the construction of the project, and to pay any additional amount by which the cost of the project exceeds the estimate, by the appropriation to the construction fund of additional municipal funds or the proceeds of additional bonds to be issued by the municipality; and</p>
          <p>(7) conformity of the project and of the loan or grant application with the state water pollution control plan as certified to the federal government and with all other conditions under applicable state and federal law for a grant of state or federal funds of the nature and in the amount involved.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.16.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Rules.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The agency shall promulgate permanent rules for the administration of grants and loans authorized to be made under the water pollution control program, which rules, however, shall not be applicable to the issuance of bonds by the commissioner of management and budget as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.17">116.17</a>. The rules shall contain as a minimum:</p>
          <p>(1) procedures for application by municipalities;</p>
          <p>(2) conditions for the administration of the grant or loan;</p>
          <p>(3) criteria for the ranking of projects in order of priority for grants or loans, based on factors including the extent and nature of pollution, technological feasibility, assurance of proper operation, maintenance and replacement, and participation in multimunicipal systems; and</p>
          <p>(4) such other matters as the agency and the commissioner find necessary to the proper administration of the grant program.</p>
          <p>(b) The agency shall award the amount of additional priority points necessary to place a project in the fundable range of the intended use plan if the agency determines that the project would repair a facility that is an imminent threat to discharge untreated or partially treated sewage to the Boundary Waters Canoe Area Wilderness if it fails.</p>
          <p>(c) For purposes of awarding independent state grants, the agency may by rule waive the federal 20-year planning requirement for municipalities with a population of less than 1,500.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.16.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/597/#laws.0.55.0">1984 c 597 s 55</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.16.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/597/#laws.0.55.0">1984 c 597 s 55</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.16.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Loans.</span></h2>
          <p>Each loan made to a municipality from the proceeds of state bonds, when authorized by law, shall be evidenced by resolutions adopted by the agency and by the governing body of the municipality, obligating the municipality to repay the loan to the commissioner of management and budget, for credit to the water pollution control bond account in the state bond fund, in annual installments including both principal and interest, each in an amount sufficient to pay the principal amount within such period as may be provided by the agency in accordance with the law authorizing the loan, with interest on the declining balance thereof at a rate not less than the average annual interest rate on state bonds of the issue from the proceeds of which the loan was made, and obligating the municipality to provide money for such repayment from user charges, taxes, special assessments, or other funds available to it. For the purpose of repaying such loans the municipality by resolution of its governing body may undertake to fix rates and charges for disposal system service and enter into contracts for the payment by others of costs of construction, maintenance, and use of the project in accordance with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/444.075">444.075</a>, and may pledge the revenues derived therefrom, and the agency may condition any such loans upon the establishment of rates and charges or the execution of contracts sufficient to produce the revenues pledged.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.16.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Applications.</span></h2>
          <p>Applications by municipalities for grants or loans under the water pollution control program shall be made to the authority on forms requiring information prescribed by rules of the agency. The authority shall send the application to the agency within ten days of receipt. The commissioner shall certify to the authority those applications which appear to meet the criteria set forth in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.16">116.16</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.18">116.18</a> and the rules promulgated hereunder, and the authority shall award the grants or loans on the basis of the criteria and priorities established by the agency in its rules and in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.16">116.16</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.18">116.18</a>. A municipality that is designated under agency rules to receive state or federal funding for a project and that does not make a timely application for or that refuses the funding is not eligible for either state or federal funding for that project in that fiscal year or the subsequent year.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.16.9a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9a.<span class="headnote">Subsequent grants.</span></h2>
          <p>A municipality awarded a final grant of funding for a project under the program established by the 1972 Federal Water Pollution Control Act amendments or the state independent grants program is not eligible for additional funding to replace that project under the federal program or the state program, unless the funding is necessary as a result of subsequent changes in state water quality standards, effluent limits, or technical design requirements, or for a municipality awarded the final grant before October 1, 1984, if the funding is necessary for the provision of increased capacity.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.16.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Costs.</span></h2>
          <p>To the extent the agency administers or engages in activities necessary for administering any aspects of the Federal Water Pollution Control Act as amended, United States Code, title 33, section 1251 et seq., the agency may assess the costs of such administrative activities, in an amount not to exceed that allowed by federal law, against the federal construction grant funds allotted to the state.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.16.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.<span class="headnote">Awarding grants and loans.</span></h2>
          <p>Upon certification by the commissioner of the Pollution Control Agency, the authority shall notify a municipality that is to receive a grant or loan and advise the municipality of the grant agreement or loan form or other document that must be executed to complete the grant or loan. Upon certification from the commissioner that the work has been completed and that payment is proper, the authority shall pay to the municipality the periodic grant or loan payment.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.16.12">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 12.<span class="headnote">Amendments.</span></h2>
          <p>A municipality that seeks an amendment to a previously awarded grant or loan shall follow the procedure in subdivision 9 for applying to the authority. The request for a grant or loan amendment must be forwarded by the authority to the commissioner of the Pollution Control Agency for consideration, and the authority shall process a grant or loan amendment that is approved by the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.16.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1971/1/20/#laws.0.1.0">Ex1971 c 20 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/123/#laws.5.7.0">1973 c 123 art 5 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/423/#laws.0.1.0">1973 c 423 s 1</a>-6; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/492/#laws.0.14.0">1973 c 492 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/2/#laws.0.53.0">1976 c 2 s 53</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/76/#laws.0.5.0">1976 c 76 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1977/0/418/#laws.0.1.0">1977 c 418 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/397/#laws.0.1.0">1980 c 397 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/509/#laws.0.27.0">1980 c 509 s 27</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/301/#laws.0.115.0">1983 c 301 s 115</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/597/#laws.0.42.0">1984 c 597 s 42</a>-46; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/640/#laws.0.32.0">1984 c 640 s 32</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/1/14/#laws.19.1.0">1Sp1985 c 14 art 19 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/186/#laws.0.15.0">1987 c 186 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/386/#laws.3.1.0">1987 c 386 art 3 s 1</a>-6; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/271/#laws.0.16.0">1989 c 271 s 16</a>-21; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/564/#laws.0.1.0">1990 c 564 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/628/#laws.3.8.0">1994 c 628 art 3 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/233/#laws.2.56.0">1995 c 233 art 2 s 56</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/404/#laws.0.37.0">1998 c 404 s 37</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/112/#laws.2.16.0">2003 c 112 art 2 s 16</a>,50; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/101/#laws.2.109.0">2009 c 101 art 2 s 109</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116.162"><b>116.162</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/463/#laws.0.61.0">1996 c 463 s 61</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116.163">
        <h1 class="shn">116.163 AGENCY REVIEW; FINANCIAL ASSISTANCE AND BIDS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.163.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Construction grants and loans; applications.</span></h2>
          <p>The agency shall, pursuant to agency rules and within 90 days of receipt of a completed application for a wastewater treatment facility construction grant or loan, grant or deny the application and notify the municipality of the agency's decision. The time for consideration of the application by the agency may be extended up to 180 days if the municipality and the agency agree it is necessary.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.163.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Planning time limited.</span></h2>
          <p>A municipality shall complete all planning work required by the agency for award of a grant or loan, and be ready to advertise for bids for construction, within two years of receipt of grant or loan funds under subdivision 1. The planning time may be extended automatically by the amount of time the agency exceeds its 90-day review under subdivision 1.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.163.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Bid review.</span></h2>
          <p>After a municipality has accepted bids for construction of a wastewater treatment project, the agency must review the bids within 30 days of receipt.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.163.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/465/#laws.3.4.0">1986 c 465 art 3 s 4</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116.165">
        <h1 class="shn">116.165 INSPECTION RESPONSIBILITY.</h1>
        <p>When a wastewater treatment plant is constructed with federal funds and a federal agency conducts inspections of the plant, the owner of the plant or the owner's designee must conduct inspections and forward all inspection documents required by the agency to the agency for its review.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.165.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/465/#laws.3.5.0">1986 c 465 art 3 s 5</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116.167"><b>116.167</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/386/#laws.3.30.0">1987 c 386 art 3 s 30</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116.17">
        <h1 class="shn">116.17 MINNESOTA STATE WATER POLLUTION CONTROL BONDS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.17.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Purpose and appropriation.</span></h2>
          <p>For the purpose of providing money to be appropriated or loaned to municipalities under the Minnesota state water pollution control program for the acquisition and betterment of public land, buildings, and improvements of a capital nature needed for the prevention, control, and abatement of water pollution in accordance with the provisions of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.16">116.16</a>, when such appropriations or loans are authorized by law and funds therefor are requested by the agency, the commissioner of management and budget shall sell and issue bonds of the state of Minnesota for the prompt and full payment of which, with interest thereon, the full faith, credit, and taxing powers of the state are irrevocably pledged. Bonds shall be issued pursuant to this section only as authorized by a law specifying the purpose thereof and the maximum amount of the proceeds authorized to be expended for this purpose. Any act authorizing the issuance of bonds for this purpose, together with this section, constitutes complete authority for such issue, and such bonds shall not be subject to restrictions or limitations contained in any other law.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.17.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Issuing bonds.</span></h2>
          <p>Upon request by resolution of the agency and upon authorization as provided in subdivision 1 the commissioner of management and budget shall sell and issue Minnesota state water pollution control bonds in the aggregate amount requested, upon sealed bids and upon such notice, at such price, in such form and denominations, bearing interest at a rate or rates, maturing in amounts and on dates, with or without option of prepayment upon notice and at specified times and prices, payable at a bank or banks within or outside the state, with provisions, if any, for registration, conversion, and exchange and for the issuance of temporary bonds or notes in anticipation of the sale or delivery of definitive bonds, and in accordance with further provisions, as the commissioner of management and budget shall determine, subject to the approval of the attorney general, but not subject to chapter 14, including section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.386">14.386</a>. The bonds shall be executed by the commissioner of management and budget under official seal. The signature of the commissioner on the bonds and any appurtenant interest coupons and the seal may be printed, lithographed, engraved, stamped, or otherwise reproduced thereon, except that each bond shall be authenticated by the manual signature on its face of the commissioner or of an authorized representative of a bank designated by the commissioner as registrar or other authenticating agent. The commissioner of management and budget shall ascertain and certify to the purchasers of the bonds the performance and existence of all acts, conditions, and things necessary to make them valid and binding general obligations of the state of Minnesota, subject to the approval of the attorney general.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.17.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Expenses.</span></h2>
          <p>All expenses incidental to the sale, printing, execution, and delivery of bonds pursuant to this section, including but not limited to actual and necessary travel and subsistence expenses of state officers and employees for such purposes, and any expenses of litigation relating to the validity of the bonds, shall be paid from the bond proceeds fund, and the amounts necessary therefor are appropriated from that fund; provided that if any amount is specifically appropriated for this purpose in an act authorizing the issuance of bonds pursuant to this section, such expenses shall be limited to the amount so appropriated.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.17.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">State water pollution control bond account.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of management and budget shall maintain in the state bond fund a separate bookkeeping account which shall be designated as the state water pollution control bond account, to record receipts and disbursements of money transferred to the fund to pay Minnesota state water pollution control bonds and income from the investment of such money, which income shall be credited to the account in each fiscal year in an amount equal to the approximate average return that year on all funds invested by the commissioner of management and budget, as determined by the commissioner of management and budget, times the average balance in the account that year.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.17.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Appropriations to bond account.</span></h2>
          <p>The premium and accrued interest received on each issue of Minnesota state water pollution control bonds, and all loan payments received under the provisions of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.16#stat.116.16.5">116.16, subdivision 5</a>, shall be credited to the bond account. All income from the investment of Minnesota state water pollution control bond proceeds, shall also be credited to the bond account. In order to reduce the amount of taxes otherwise required to be levied, there shall also be credited to the bond account therein from the general fund in the state treasury, on November 1 in each year, a sum of money sufficient in amount, when added to the balance then on hand therein, to pay all Minnesota water pollution control bonds and interest thereon due and to become due to and including July 1 in the second ensuing year. All money so credited and all income from the investment thereof is annually appropriated to the bond account for the payment of such bonds and interest thereon, and shall be available in the bond account prior to the levy of the tax in any year required by the constitution, article XI, section 7. The commissioner of management and budget is directed to make the appropriate entries in the accounts of the respective funds.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.17.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Tax levy.</span></h2>
          <p>On or before December 1 in each year the state auditor shall levy on all taxable property within the state whatever tax may be necessary to produce an amount sufficient, with all money then and theretofore credited to the bond account, to pay the entire amount of principal and interest then and theretofore due and principal and interest to become due on or before July 1 in the second year thereafter on Minnesota water pollution control bonds. This tax shall be subject to no limitation of rate or amount until all such bonds and interest thereon are fully paid. The proceeds of this tax are appropriated and shall be credited to the state bond fund, and the principal of and interest on the bonds are payable from such proceeds, and the whole thereof, or so much as may be necessary, is appropriated for such payments. If at any time there is insufficient money from the proceeds of such taxes to pay the principal and interest when due on Minnesota water pollution control bonds, such principal and interest shall be paid out of the general fund in the state treasury, and the amount necessary therefor is hereby appropriated.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.17.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1971/1/20/#laws.0.2.0">Ex1971 c 20 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/423/#laws.0.7.0">1973 c 423 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/492/#laws.0.14.0">1973 c 492 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/2/#laws.0.172.0">1976 c 2 s 172</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/424/#laws.0.130.0">1982 c 424 s 130</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/301/#laws.0.116.0">1983 c 301 s 116</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/1/14/#laws.4.14.0">1Sp1985 c 14 art 4 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/271/#laws.0.22.0">1989 c 271 s 22</a>-24; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/233/#laws.2.56.0">1995 c 233 art 2 s 56</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/187/#laws.5.14.0">1997 c 187 art 5 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/112/#laws.2.17.0">2003 c 112 art 2 s 17</a>,50; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/101/#laws.2.109.0">2009 c 101 art 2 s 109</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116.18">
        <h1 class="shn">116.18 WATER POLLUTION CONTROL FUNDS; APPROPRIATIONS AND BONDS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.18.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Appropriation from bond proceeds fund.</span></h2>
          <p>The sum of $167,000,000, or so much thereof as may be necessary, is appropriated from the bond proceeds fund in the state treasury to the Pollution Control Agency, for the period commencing on July 23, 1971, to be granted and disbursed to municipalities and agencies of the state in aid of the construction of projects conforming to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.16">116.16</a>, in accordance with the rules, priorities, and criteria therein described.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.18.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/1/14/#laws.19.38.0">1Sp1985 c 14 art 19 s 38</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.18.2a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2a.<span class="headnote">State matching grants program.</span></h2>
          <p>For projects tendered, on or after October 1, 1987, a grant of federal money under section 201(g), section 202, 203, or 206(f) of the Federal Water Pollution Control Act, as amended, United States Code, title 33, sections 1251 to 1376, at 55 percent or more of the eligible cost for construction of the treatment works, state money appropriated under subdivision 1 must be expended for 50 percent of the nonfederal share of the eligible cost of construction for municipalities with populations of 25,000 or less.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.18.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/423/#laws.0.10.0">1973 c 423 s 10</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.18.3a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3a.<span class="headnote">State independent grants program.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The Public Facilities Authority must adopt the objective of maintaining financial assistance to municipalities that the agency has listed on its annual municipal project list of approximately 50 percent of the eligible cost of construction for municipalities with populations over 25,000 and 80 percent of the eligible cost for municipalities with populations of 25,000 or less. Financial assistance may be provided by the Public Facilities Authority through a combination of low interest loans under the state revolving fund under chapter 446A, independent state grants, and other financial assistance available to the municipality. The Public Facilities Authority may award independent grants for projects certified by the state pollution control commissioner for 35 percent or, if the population of the municipality is 25,000 or less, 65 percent of the eligible cost of construction. These grants may be awarded in separate steps for planning and design in addition to actual construction. Not more than $2,000,000 of the total amount of grants awarded under this subdivision in any single fiscal year may be awarded to a single grantee.</p>
          <p>(b) Up to $1,000,000 of the money to be awarded as grants under this subdivision in any single fiscal year shall be set aside for municipalities having substantial economic development projects that cannot come to fruition without municipal wastewater treatment improvements. The agency shall forward its municipal needs list to the authority at the beginning of each fiscal year, and the authority shall review the list and identify those municipalities having substantial economic development projects. After the available money is allocated to municipalities in accordance with agency priorities, the set-aside shall be used by the authority to award grants to remaining municipalities that have been identified.</p>
          <p>(c) Grants may also be awarded under this subdivision to reimburse municipalities willing to proceed with projects and be reimbursed in a subsequent year at the grant percentage determined in paragraph (a).</p>
          <p>(d) Municipalities that entered into an intent to award agreement with the agency under paragraph (c), in the state fiscal years 1985 to 1988, will be reimbursed at 55 percent or, if the population of the municipality is 25,000 or less, 85 percent of the eligible cost of construction.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.18.3b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3b.<span class="headnote">Capital cost component grant.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The definitions of "capital cost component," "capital cost component grant," "service fee," "service contract," and "private vendor" in Minnesota Statutes 2012, section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/471A.02">471A.02</a>, apply to this subdivision.</p>
          <p>(b) Beginning in fiscal year 1989, up to $1,500,000 of the money to be awarded as grants under subdivision 3a in any single fiscal year may be set aside for the award of capital cost component grants to municipalities on the municipal needs list for part of the capital cost component of the service fee under a service contract for a term of at least 20 years with a private vendor for the purpose of constructing and operating wastewater treatment facilities.</p>
          <p>(c) The amount granted to a municipality shall be 50 percent of the average total eligible costs of municipalities of similar size recently awarded state and federal grants under the provisions of subdivisions 2a and 3a and the Federal Water Pollution Control Act, United States Code, title 33, sections 1281 to 1299. Federal and state eligibility requirements for determining the amount of grant dollars to be awarded to a municipality are not applicable to municipalities awarded capital cost component grants. Federal and state eligibility requirements for determining which cities qualify for state and federal grants are applicable, except as provided in this subdivision.</p>
          <p>(d) Except as provided in this subdivision, municipalities receiving capital cost component grants shall not be required to comply with federal and state regulations regarding facilities planning and procurement contained in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.16">116.16</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.18">116.18</a>, except those necessary to issue a national pollutant discharge elimination system permit or state disposal system permit and those necessary to assure that the proposed facilities are reasonably capable of meeting the conditions of the permit over 20 years. The municipality and the private vendor shall be parties to the permit. Municipalities receiving capital cost component grants may also be exempted by rules of the agency from other state and federal regulations relating to the award of state and federal grants for wastewater treatment facilities, except those necessary to protect the state from fraud or misuse of state funds.</p>
          <p>(e) Funds shall be distributed from the set-aside to municipalities that apply for the funds in accordance with these provisions in the order of their ranking on the municipal needs list.</p>
          <p>(f) The authority shall award capital cost component grants to municipalities selected by the state pollution control commissioner upon certification by the state pollution control commissioner that the municipalities' projects and applications have been reviewed and approved in accordance with this subdivision and agency rules adopted under paragraph (g).</p>
          <p>(g) The agency shall adopt permanent rules to provide for the administration of grants awarded under this subdivision.</p>
          <p>(h) The commissioner of employment and economic development may adopt rules containing procedures for administration of the authority's duties as set forth in paragraph (f).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.18.3c">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3c.<span class="headnote">Individual on-site treatment systems and alternative discharging sewage systems; grant program.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Beginning in fiscal year 1989, up to ten percent of the money to be awarded as grants under subdivision 3a in any single fiscal year, up to a maximum of $1,000,000, may be set aside for the award of grants by the agency to municipalities to reimburse owners of individual on-site wastewater treatment systems or alternative discharging sewage systems for a part of the costs of upgrading or replacing the systems.</p>
          <p>(b) An individual on-site treatment system is a wastewater treatment system, or part thereof, that uses soil treatment and disposal technology to treat 5,000 gallons or less of wastewater per day from dwellings or other establishments.</p>
          <p>(c) An alternative discharging sewage system is a system permitted under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115.58">115.58</a> that:</p>
          <p>(1) serves one or more dwellings and other establishments;</p>
          <p>(2) discharges less than 10,000 gallons of water per day; and</p>
          <p>(3) uses any treatment and disposal methods other than subsurface soil treatment and disposal.</p>
          <p>(d) Municipalities may apply yearly for grants of up to 50 percent of the cost of replacing or upgrading individual on-site treatment systems, including conversion to an alternative discharging sewage system, within their jurisdiction, up to a limit of $5,000 per system or per connection to a cluster system. Before agency approval of the grant application, a municipality must certify that:</p>
          <p>(1) it has adopted and is enforcing the requirements of Minnesota Rules governing subsurface sewage treatment systems;</p>
          <p>(2) the existing systems for which application is made do not conform to those rules, are at least 20 years old, do not serve seasonal residences, and were not constructed with state or federal funds; and</p>
          <p>(3) the costs requested do not include administrative costs, costs for improvements or replacements made before the application is submitted to the agency unless it pertains to the plan finally adopted, and planning and engineering costs other than those for the individual site evaluations and system design.</p>
          <p>(e) The federal and state regulations regarding the award of state and federal wastewater treatment grants do not apply to municipalities or systems funded under this subdivision, except as provided in this subdivision.</p>
          <p>(f) The agency shall adopt permanent rules regarding priorities, distribution of funds, payments, inspections, procedures for administration of the agency's duties, and other matters that the agency finds necessary for proper administration of grants awarded under this subdivision.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.18.3d">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3d.<span class="headnote">Adjusting grants.</span></h2>
          <p>A municipality with a population of 25,000 or less that was tendered a state matching grant under subdivision 2a, or a state independent grant under subdivision 3a, or a federal grant under the Federal Water Pollution Control Act, United States Code, title 33, sections 1281 to 1299, from October 1, 1984, through September 30, 1987, shall, after the municipality has awarded bids for construction of the treatment works, and upon request, receive a grant increase of 2.5 percent of the total eligible costs of construction, up to the maximum entitlement for grants awarded on or after October 1, 1987, under subdivisions 2a and 3a. The municipality must inform other entities that are providing funding for construction of the treatment works of the grant increase, and repay any funds to which it is not entitled. A municipality must not receive funding for more than 100 percent of the total costs of the treatment works. Documentation of money received from other sources must be submitted with the request for the grant increase. Money remaining after all grants have been awarded under this subdivision may be used for the award of grants under subdivisions 2a and 3a. An adjustment grant awarded after July 1, 1989, that is a continuation of a previously awarded adjustment grant must be awarded through a letter from the agency to the municipality stating the grant amount. A formal grant agreement is not required.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.18.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Bond authorization.</span></h2>
          <p>For the purpose of providing money appropriated in subdivision 1 for grants to municipalities and agencies of the state for the acquisition and betterment of public land, buildings, and improvements of a capital nature needed for the prevention, control, and abatement of water pollution, the commissioner of management and budget is authorized upon request of the Pollution Control Agency to sell and issue Minnesota state water pollution control bonds in the amount of $156,000,000, in the manner and upon the conditions prescribed in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.17">116.17</a> and in the constitution, article XI, sections 4 to 7. The proceeds of the bonds, except as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.17#stat.116.17.5">116.17, subdivision 5</a>, are appropriated and shall be credited to a Minnesota state water pollution control account in the bond proceeds fund. The amount of bonds issued pursuant to this authorization shall not exceed at any time the amount needed to produce a balance in the water pollution control account equal to the aggregate amount of grants then approved and not previously disbursed, plus the amount of grants to be approved in the current and the following fiscal year, as estimated by the Pollution Control Agency.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.18.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Federal and other funds.</span></h2>
          <p>All federal and other funds made available for any purpose of the water pollution control program are also appropriated for the program.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.18.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Continued appropriations.</span></h2>
          <p>None of the appropriations made in this section shall lapse until the purpose for which it is made has been accomplished or abandoned. The amount of each grant approved for the water pollution control program shall be and remain appropriated for that purpose until the grant is fully disbursed or part or all thereof is revoked by the Pollution Control Agency.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.18.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1971/1/20/#laws.0.3.0">Ex1971 c 20 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/423/#laws.0.8.0">1973 c 423 s 8</a>,9; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/492/#laws.0.14.0">1973 c 492 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/771/#laws.0.1.0">1973 c 771 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/354/#laws.0.1.0">1975 c 354 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/2/#laws.0.172.0">1976 c 2 s 172</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1977/0/418/#laws.0.2.0">1977 c 418 s 2</a>,3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1979/0/285/#laws.0.1.0">1979 c 285 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/361/#laws.0.14.0">1981 c 361 s 14</a>,15; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/301/#laws.0.117.0">1983 c 301 s 117</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/597/#laws.0.47.0">1984 c 597 s 47</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/1/14/#laws.19.4.0">1Sp1985 c 14 art 19 s 4</a>-6; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/186/#laws.0.15.0">1987 c 186 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/277/#laws.0.1.0">1987 c 277 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/312/#laws.1.26.0">1987 c 312 art 1 s 26</a> subd 2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/386/#laws.3.7.0">1987 c 386 art 3 s 7</a>,8; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/686/#laws.1.59.0">1988 c 686 art 1 s 59</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/271/#laws.0.25.0">1989 c 271 s 25</a>-28; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/300/#laws.1.28.0">1989 c 300 art 1 s 28</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/354/#laws.0.1.0">1989 c 354 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/564/#laws.0.3.0">1990 c 564 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/180/#laws.0.5.0">1993 c 180 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/246/#laws.0.13.0">1997 c 246 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/401/#laws.0.44.0">1998 c 401 s 44</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/404/#laws.0.38.0">1998 c 404 s 38</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/4/#laws.0.1.0">1Sp2003 c 4 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/101/#laws.2.109.0">2009 c 101 art 2 s 109</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/109/#laws.0.14.0">2009 c 109 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/258/#laws.0.1.0">2014 c 258 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116.181"><b>116.181</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/248/#laws.0.19.0">2014 c 248 s 19</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116.182">
        <h1 class="shn">116.182 FINANCIAL ASSISTANCE PROGRAM.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.182.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) For the purposes of this section, the terms defined in this subdivision have the meanings given them.</p>
          <p>(b) "Agency" means the Pollution Control Agency.</p>
          <p>(c) "Authority" means the Public Facilities Authority established in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/446A.03">446A.03</a>.</p>
          <p>(d) "Commissioner" means the commissioner of the Pollution Control Agency.</p>
          <p>(e) "Essential project components" means those components of a wastewater disposal system that are necessary to convey or treat a municipality's existing wastewater flows and loadings.</p>
          <p>(f) "Municipality" means a county, home rule charter or statutory city, town, the Metropolitan Council, an Indian tribe or an authorized Indian tribal organization; or any other governmental subdivision of the state responsible by law for the prevention, control, and abatement of water pollution in any area of the state.</p>
          <p>(g) "Outstanding international resource value waters" are the surface waters of the state in the Lake Superior Basin, other than class 7 waters and those waters designated as outstanding resource value waters.</p>
          <p>(h) "Outstanding resource value waters" are those that have high water quality, wilderness characteristics, unique scientific or ecological significance, exceptional recreation value, or other special qualities that warrant special protection.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.182.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Applicability.</span></h2>
          <p>This section governs the commissioner's certification of projects seeking financial assistance under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103F.725#stat.103F.725.1a">103F.725, subdivision 1a</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/446A.07">446A.07</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/446A.072">446A.072</a>; or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/446A.073">446A.073</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.182.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Project review.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall review a municipality's proposed project to determine whether it meets the criteria in this section and the rules adopted under this section. The review must include a determination of the essential project components for wastewater treatment projects.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.182.3a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3a.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/248/#laws.0.19.0">2014 c 248 s 19</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.182.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Certifying approved projects.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall certify to the authority each approved project, including for wastewater treatment projects a statement of the essential project components and associated costs.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.182.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Rules.</span></h2>
          <p> The agency shall adopt rules for the administration of the financial assistance program. For wastewater treatment projects, the rules must include:</p>
          <p>(1) application requirements;</p>
          <p>(2) criteria for the ranking of projects in order of priority based on factors including the type of project and the degree of environmental impact, and scenic and wild river standards; and</p>
          <p>(3) criteria for determining essential project components.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.182.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Transferring money.</span></h2>
          <p>As the projects in the programs specified under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.18">116.18</a>, except the program under subdivision 3c of that section, are completed, any amounts remaining from appropriations for the programs are appropriated to the authority for the wastewater infrastructure funding program in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/446A.072">446A.072</a>, provided this use of the funds does not violate applicable provisions of any bond or note resolutions, indentures, or other instruments, contracts, or agreements associated with the source of the funds.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.182.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/601/#laws.0.10.0">1992 c 601 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/628/#laws.3.9.0">1994 c 628 art 3 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/632/#laws.2.32.0">1994 c 632 art 2 s 32</a>-35; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/463/#laws.0.60.0">1996 c 463 s 60</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/404/#laws.0.39.0">1998 c 404 s 39</a>,40; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/86/#laws.1.24.0">1999 c 86 art 1 s 24</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/492/#laws.1.44.0">2000 c 492 art 1 s 44</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/20/#laws.1.32.0">2005 c 20 art 1 s 32</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/96/#laws.2.1.0">2007 c 96 art 2 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/125/#laws.1.26.0">2013 c 125 art 1 s 26</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116.19"><b>116.19</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/379/#laws.1.114.0">2002 c 379 art 1 s 114</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116.195">
        <h1 class="shn">116.195 BENEFICIAL USE OF WASTEWATER AND STORMWATER; CAPITAL GRANTS FOR DEMONSTRATION PROJECTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.195.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) For the purposes of this section, the following terms have the meanings given them.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) "Agency" means the Pollution Control Agency.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) "Beneficial use of wastewater or stormwater" means:</p>
          <p>(1) use of the effluent from a wastewater treatment plant that replaces use of groundwater; or</p>
          <p>(2) use of stormwater that replaces the use of groundwater.</p>
          <p class="in">(d) "Capital project" means the acquisition or betterment of public land, buildings, and other public improvements of a capital nature for the treatment of wastewater intended for beneficial use or for the use of stormwater to replace groundwater use. Capital project includes projects to retrofit, expand, or construct new treatment facilities.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.195.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Grants for capital project design.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">The agency shall make grant awards to political subdivisions for up to 50 percent of the costs to predesign and design capital projects that demonstrate the beneficial use of wastewater or stormwater. The grant agreement must provide that the predesign and design work being funded is public information and available to anyone without charge. The agency must make the predesign and design work available on its website.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.195.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Grants for capital project implementation.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) The agency shall make grant awards to political subdivisions for up to 50 percent of the costs to acquire, construct, install, furnish, and equip capital projects that demonstrate the beneficial use of wastewater or stormwater. The political subdivision must submit design plans and specifications to the agency as part of the application.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) The agency must consult with the Public Facilities Authority and the commissioner of natural resources in reviewing and ranking applications for grants under this section.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) The application must identify the uses of the treated wastewater or stormwater and greater weight will be given to applications that include a binding commitment to participate by the user or users.</p>
          <p class="in">(d) The agency must give preference to projects that will reduce use of the greatest volume of groundwater from aquifers with the slowest rate of recharge.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.195.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Application form; procedures.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">The agency shall develop an application form and procedures.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.195.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/248/#laws.0.19.0">2014 c 248 s 19</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.195.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/179/#laws.0.37.0">2008 c 179 s 37</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/1/6/#laws.2.22.0">1Sp2011 c 6 art 2 s 22</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116.201"><b>116.201</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/137/#laws.2.23.0">2013 c 137 art 2 s 23</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116.202">
        <h1 class="shn">116.202 COAL TAR SEALANT; USE AND SALE PROHIBITED.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.202.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>The following terms have the meanings given.</p>
          <p>(a) "Coal tar sealant product" means a surface applied sealing product containing coal tar, coal tar pitch, coal tar pitch volatiles, or any variation assigned the Chemical Abstracts Service (CAS) number 65996-93-2, 65996-89-6, or 8007-45-2.</p>
          <p>(b) "Commissioner" means the commissioner of the Pollution Control Agency.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.202.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Use prohibited.</span></h2>
          <p>Except as provided in subdivision 4, a person shall not apply coal tar sealant products on asphalt-paved surfaces.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.202.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Sale prohibited.</span></h2>
          <p>Except as provided in subdivision 4, a person shall not sell a coal tar sealant product that is formulated or marketed for application on asphalt-paved surfaces.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.202.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Exemptions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner may exempt a person from this section if the commissioner determines that one or both of the following apply:</p>
          <p>(1) the person is researching the effects of a coal tar sealant product on the environment; or</p>
          <p>(2) the person is developing an alternative technology and the use of a coal tar sealant product is required for research or development.</p>
          <p>(b) A request for exemption must be made to the commissioner in writing, including an explanation of why the exemption is needed for research or the development of an alternative technology.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.202.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Compliance and enforcement.</span></h2>
          <p>Local units of government may adopt by reference and enforce the provisions of this section. The commissioner may provide technical support to local units of government for compliance and enforcement of this section. The commissioner may respond to compliance and enforcement cases transcending jurisdictional boundaries, cases requiring statewide corrective actions, or requests for assistance or referral from local units of government.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.202.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/137/#laws.2.17.0">2013 c 137 art 2 s 17</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116.2021">
        <h1 class="shn">116.2021 STATE SALT PURCHASE REPORT AND REDUCTION GOAL.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.2021.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definition.</span></h2>
          <p>For the purposes of this section, "deicing salt" refers to salt in its solid form used to melt snow and ice, excluding salt used on roads managed by the Department of Transportation.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.2021.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Salt purchase report.</span></h2>
          <p>By February 1, 2025, and every year thereafter, the commissioner of the Pollution Control Agency, in cooperation with other state agencies, must submit a report to the chairs and ranking minority members of the legislative committees and divisions with jurisdiction over environment and natural resources policy and finance that details the purchase of deicing salt by state agencies, excluding the Department of Transportation, and strategies to meet the salt reduction goal established in subdivision 3.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.2021.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Reduction goal.</span></h2>
          <p>It is the goal of the state that no later than January 1, 2030, state agencies will reduce the purchase of deicing salt by 25 percent from the level first reported under subdivision 2.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.2021.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Sunset.</span></h2>
          <p>This section expires January 1, 2030.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.2021.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/116/#laws.2.19.0">2024 c 116 art 2 s 19</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116.2022">
        <h1 class="shn">116.2022 STATE NITROGEN FERTILIZER PURCHASE REPORT AND REDUCTION GOAL.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.2022.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Nitrogen fertilizer report.</span></h2>
          <p>By February 1, 2025, and every year thereafter, the commissioner of the Pollution Control Agency, in cooperation with other state agencies, must submit a report to the chairs and ranking minority members of the legislative committees and divisions with jurisdiction over environment and natural resources policy and finance that details the purchase of nitrogen fertilizer by state agencies and strategies to meet the nitrogen fertilizer reduction goal established in subdivision 2.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.2022.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Reduction goal.</span></h2>
          <p>It is the goal of the state that no later than January 1, 2030, state agencies will reduce the purchase of nitrogen fertilizer by 25 percent from the level first reported under subdivision 1.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.2022.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Sunset.</span></h2>
          <p>This section expires January 1, 2030.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.2022.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/116/#laws.2.20.0">2024 c 116 art 2 s 20</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">NUTRIENTS IN CLEANING AGENTS AND WATER CONDITIONERS</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116.21">
        <h1 class="shn">116.21 NUTRIENTS IN CLEANING AGENTS AND WATER CONDITIONERS; CONTROL; STATEMENT OF POLICY.</h1>
        <p>The legislature seeks to encourage the Minnesota Pollution Control Agency through the passage of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.21">116.21</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.35">116.35</a>, to set standards limiting the amount of nutrients in various cleaning agents and water conditioning agents. The legislature realizes that the nutrients contained in many of these products serve a valuable purpose in increasing their overall effectiveness, but we are also aware that they overstimulate the growth of aquatic life and eventually lead to an acceleration of the natural eutrophication process of our state's waters. Limitations imposed under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.21">116.21</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.35">116.35</a> should, however, be made taking the following factors into consideration:</p>
        <p>(1) the availability of safe, nonpolluting, and effective substitutes;</p>
        <p>(2) the difference in the mineral content of water in various parts of the state; and</p>
        <p>(3) the differing needs of industrial, commercial and household users of cleaning agents and chemical water conditioners.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.21.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1971/0/896/#laws.0.1.0">1971 c 896 s 1</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116.22">
        <h1 class="shn">116.22 DEFINITIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.22.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Applicability.</span></h2>
          <p>For purposes of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.21">116.21</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.35">116.35</a>, the terms defined in this section shall have the meanings given them.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.22.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>MS 1990 [Renumbered subd 3]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.22.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Chemical water conditioner.</span></h2>
          <p>"Chemical water conditioner" means a water softening chemical, antiscale chemical, corrosion inhibitor or other substance intended to be used to treat water.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.22.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.</h2>
          <p>MS 1990 [Renumbered subd 4]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.22.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Cleaning agent.</span></h2>
          <p>"Cleaning agent" means a laundry detergent, dishwashing compound, household cleaner, metal cleaner, degreasing compound, commercial cleaner, industrial cleaner, phosphate compound or other substance intended to be used for cleaning purposes.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.22.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.</h2>
          <p>MS 1990 [Renumbered subd 2]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.22.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Nutrient.</span></h2>
          <p>"Nutrient" means a substance or combination of substances which, if added to waters in sufficient quantities, provides nourishment that promotes growth of aquatic vegetation in densities which:</p>
          <p>(1) interfere with use of the waters by humans or by any animal, fish or plant useful to humans; or</p>
          <p>(2) contribute to degradation or alteration of the quality of the waters to an extent detrimental to their use by humans or by any animal, fish or plant that is useful to humans.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.22.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1971/0/896/#laws.0.2.0">1971 c 896 s 2</a>; 1986 c 444</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116.23">
        <h1 class="shn">116.23 PROHIBITION AND RESTRICTIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.23.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Nutrient concentrations.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">No person shall manufacture for use or sale in Minnesota or import into Minnesota for resale any cleaning agent or chemical water conditioner which contains a prescribed nutrient in a concentration that is greater than the prescribed maximum permissible concentration of that nutrient in that cleaning agent or chemical water conditioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.23.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Residential dishwasher detergent.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">No person shall sell, distribute, offer, or expose for sale at retail any household dishwasher detergent that contains more than 0.5 percent phosphorus by weight. This subdivision does not apply to the sale or distribution of detergents for commercial or institutional dishwashing purposes.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.23.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1971/0/896/#laws.0.3.0">1971 c 896 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/131/#laws.1.76.0">2007 c 131 art 1 s 76</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116.24">
        <h1 class="shn">116.24 RULES.</h1>
        <p>The Pollution Control Agency may make rules:</p>
        <p>(1) prescribing for the purpose of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.23">116.23</a> nutrients and the maximum permissible concentration if any, of a prescribed nutrient in any cleaning agent or chemical water conditioner;</p>
        <p>(2) respecting the manner in which the concentration of any prescribed nutrient in a cleaning agent or chemical water conditioner shall be determined; and</p>
        <p>(3) requiring persons who manufacture in Minnesota any cleaning agent or chemical water conditioner to maintain books and records necessary for the proper enforcement of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.21">116.21</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.35">116.35</a> and rules thereunder, and to submit samples of cleaning agents or water conditioners to the Pollution Control Agency.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.24.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1971/0/896/#laws.0.4.0">1971 c 896 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/248/#laws.0.70.0">1985 c 248 s 70</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116.25">
        <h1 class="shn">116.25 SEIZURE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.25.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Seizure.</span></h2>
          <p>The Pollution Control Agency may seize a cleaning agent or chemical water conditioner which it reasonably believes was manufactured or imported in violation of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.23">116.23</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.25.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Storage.</span></h2>
          <p>A cleaning agent or chemical water conditioner seized under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.21">116.21</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.35">116.35</a>, may be kept or stored in the building or place where it was seized or may be removed to any other proper place by or at the direction of the Pollution Control Agency.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.25.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Removal; sample.</span></h2>
          <p>Except with the authority of the Pollution Control Agency, no person shall remove, alter or interfere with a cleaning agent or chemical water conditioner seized under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.21">116.21</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.35">116.35</a>, but the Pollution Control Agency shall, at the request of a person from whom it was seized, furnish a sample thereof to the person for analysis.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.25.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1971/0/896/#laws.0.5.0">1971 c 896 s 5</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116.26">
        <h1 class="shn">116.26 RESTORING SEIZED ITEMS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.26.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Applying to district court.</span></h2>
