          <p>When a cleaning agent or chemical water conditioner has been seized under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.21">116.21</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.35">116.35</a>, any person may within two months after the date of seizure, upon prior notice in accordance with subdivision 2 to the Pollution Control Agency by certified mail, apply to the district court within whose jurisdiction the seizure was made for an order of restoration under subdivision 3.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.26.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Notice.</span></h2>
          <p>Notice under subdivision 1 shall be mailed at least 15 days prior to the day on which the application is to be made to the district court and shall specify:</p>
          <p>(1) the district court to which the application is to be made;</p>
          <p>(2) the place where and the time when the application is to be heard;</p>
          <p>(3) the cleaning agent or chemical water conditioner in regard to which the application is to be made; and</p>
          <p>(4) the evidence upon which the applicant relies to establish entitlement to possession of the cleaning agent or chemical water conditioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.26.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Restoring items.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Subject to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.27">116.27</a> when upon hearing, the district court is satisfied (1) that the applicant is otherwise entitled to possession of the items seized, and (2) that the items seized are not and will not be required as evidence in proceedings under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.21">116.21</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.35">116.35</a>, the court shall order that the items seized be restored forthwith to the applicant.</p>
          <p>(b) Where the court is satisfied that the applicant is otherwise entitled to possession but is not satisfied as to the necessity for retention as evidence, the court shall order restoration to the applicant (1) four months after the date of seizure if no proceedings under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.23">116.23</a> have been commenced before that time, or (2) upon the final conclusion of any such proceedings.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.26.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Disposal.</span></h2>
          <p>When no application has been made under subdivision 1 within two months from the date of seizure, or when upon application no order of restoration is made, the items seized shall be delivered to the Pollution Control Agency, which may dispose of them as it sees fit.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.26.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1971/0/896/#laws.0.6.0">1971 c 896 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1978/0/674/#laws.0.60.0">1978 c 674 s 60</a>; 1986 c 444</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116.27">
        <h1 class="shn">116.27 ADDITIONAL PROHIBITION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.27.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Phosphorus content.</span></h2>
          <p>No manufacturer, wholesaler, or retailer shall sell, possess with intent to sell, or display for sale, a household laundry or dishwashing compound, including household detergents and presoaks, unless a verified or certified test result is filed with the Pollution Control Agency stating the percentage content of phosphorus by weight contained in the product.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.27.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Tests.</span></h2>
          <p>Tests shall be conducted pursuant to the methods and procedures adopted by the federal Water Quality Administration.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.27.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1971/0/896/#laws.0.7.0">1971 c 896 s 7</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116.28">
        <h1 class="shn">116.28 LISTS REQUIRED.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.28.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Contents of product.</span></h2>
          <p>No household laundry or dishwashing compound, including household detergents and presoaks, shall be sold or displayed for sale unless the product name is on a list prominently displayed near the product display stating the phosphorus content by percentage of weight to weight of the package contents. The products shall be listed in descending order and in letters and figures not less than one half inch high and proportionately wide. No list shall be required if the Pollution Control Agency adopts and has in effect standards for maximum allowable phosphorus content of household laundry and dishwashing compounds.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.28.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Current listing; availability.</span></h2>
          <p>The Pollution Control Agency shall supply any person upon request with a current listing of household laundry and dishwashing compounds and their phosphorus contents received pursuant to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.21">116.21</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.35">116.35</a>. This list shall be updated periodically.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.28.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1971/0/896/#laws.0.8.0">1971 c 896 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/275/#laws.0.1.0">1974 c 275 s 1</a>,2</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116.29">
        <h1 class="shn">116.29 FORFEITURE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.29.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Conviction.</span></h2>
          <p>When a person is convicted of an offense under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.28">116.28</a> any cleaning agent or chemical water conditioner seized in accordance with sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.21">116.21</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.35">116.35</a> is forfeited to the Pollution Control Agency and shall be disposed of as it directs.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.29.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Consent.</span></h2>
          <p>When a cleaning agent or chemical water conditioner is seized under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.21">116.21</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.35">116.35</a>, the owner or the person in whose possession it was at the time of seizure consents in writing to its destruction, it is forfeited to the Pollution Control Agency and shall be disposed of as it directs.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.29.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1971/0/896/#laws.0.9.0">1971 c 896 s 9</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116.30"><b>116.30</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/374/#laws.0.22.0">1973 c 374 s 22</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116.31"><b>116.31</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/374/#laws.0.22.0">1973 c 374 s 22</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116.32">
        <h1 class="shn">116.32 ORDER TO REFRAIN.</h1>
        <p>If a person is convicted of an offense under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.21">116.21</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.35">116.35</a>, the court may, in addition to any punishment it may impose, order that person to refrain from any further violations of the provision of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.21">116.21</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.35">116.35</a>, or rules for the violation of which the offender has been convicted, or to cease to carry on any activity specified in the order the carrying on of which, in the opinion of the court, will or is likely to result in any further violation thereof.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.32.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1971/0/896/#laws.0.12.0">1971 c 896 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/248/#laws.0.70.0">1985 c 248 s 70</a>; 1986 c 444</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116.33">
        <h1 class="shn">116.33 PROOF OF OFFENSE.</h1>
        <p>In a prosecution for an offense under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.21">116.21</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.35">116.35</a>, it is sufficient proof of the offense to establish that it was committed by an employee or agent of the accused whether or not the employee agent is identified or has been prosecuted for the offense, unless the accused establishes that the offense was committed without the accused's knowledge or consent and that the accused exercised all due diligence to prevent its commission.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.33.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1971/0/896/#laws.0.13.0">1971 c 896 s 13</a>; 1986 c 444</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116.34">
        <h1 class="shn">116.34 TIME LIMITED FOR PROCEEDINGS.</h1>
        <p>Proceedings in respect of an offense under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.21">116.21</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.35">116.35</a>, may be instituted at any time within two years after the time when the subject matter of the proceedings arose.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.34.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1971/0/896/#laws.0.14.0">1971 c 896 s 14</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116.35">
        <h1 class="shn">116.35 TRIAL OF OFFENSES.</h1>
        <p>Any complaint or information in respect of an offense under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.21">116.21</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.35">116.35</a>, may be heard, tried or determined by a court if the accused is resident or carrying on business within the territorial jurisdiction of that court although the matter of the complaint or information did not arise in that territorial jurisdiction.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.35.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1971/0/896/#laws.0.15.0">1971 c 896 s 15</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">RESTRICTED CHEMICALS</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116.36">
        <h1 class="shn">116.36 DEFINITIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.36.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Applicability.</span></h2>
          <p>For the purposes of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.36">116.36</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.38">116.38</a>, the following terms have the meanings given.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.36.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Agency.</span></h2>
          <p>"Agency" means the Minnesota Pollution Control Agency.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.36.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Commissioner.</span></h2>
          <p>"Commissioner" means the commissioner of the Pollution Control Agency.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.36.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">PCB.</span></h2>
          <p>"PCB" means the class of organic compounds known as polychlorinated biphenyls and includes any of several compounds produced by replacing one or more hydrogen atoms on the biphenyl molecule with chlorine. PCB does not include chlorinated biphenyl compounds that have functional groups attached other than chlorine.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.36.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Person.</span></h2>
          <p>"Person" has the meaning specified in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115.01#stat.115.01.10">115.01, subdivision 10</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.36.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/344/#laws.0.1.0">1976 c 344 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1977/0/347/#laws.0.16.0">1977 c 347 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/186/#laws.0.15.0">1987 c 186 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/594/#laws.1.51.0">1990 c 594 art 1 s 51</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116.37">
        <h1 class="shn">116.37 PCB; PROHIBITED USE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.37.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Certificate of exemption.</span></h2>
          <p>Beginning January 1, 1978, no person shall use, possess, sell, purchase or manufacture PCB or any product containing PCB unless the use, possession, sale, purchase or manufacture of PCB or products containing PCB is exempted by the agency. If the agency finds after there is opportunity for a public hearing on an application presented by any person, that no substitutes or feasible alternatives are reasonably available for PCB or a product containing PCB or class of products containing PCB, it shall grant a certificate of exemption which shall clearly set out the permitted use, possession, sale or purchase of PCB or a PCB product containing PCB. If the agency grants a certificate of exemption, it shall be valid for all subsequent uses of PCB or products containing PCB if the subsequent uses are consistent with the terms and conditions of the certificate of exemption. In granting certificates of exemption the agency shall at all times consider the public health and safety threatened by the use of PCB. In the consideration of certificates of exemption for the use or replacement of existing electrical transformers and capacitors the agency shall review, but not be limited to, considerations of the safety of proven alternatives, replacement costs and rules controlling the final disposal of PCB.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.37.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Exclusion.</span></h2>
          <p>In no event shall the certificate of exemption requirement or the labeling requirement of this section apply to any individual person who purchases or otherwise acquires a product containing PCB intended for consumer use in the home, provided that the use has previously been exempted by the agency and that the use is consistent with the terms and conditions of the certificate of exemption. Wastepaper, pulp, or other wood fiber materials purchased for use within this state in the manufacture of recycled paper products are exempt from the requirements of this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.37.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Labels required.</span></h2>
          <p>Beginning July 1, 1977, no person in this state shall add PCB in the manufacture of any new item, product or material, nor shall any person in this state sell any new item, product or material to which PCB has been added unless the PCB or products containing PCB are conspicuously labeled to disclose the presence of PCB and the concentrations of PCB.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.37.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Rules.</span></h2>
          <p>The agency shall promulgate rules by January 1, 1977, governing the granting of certificates of exemption and the requirements of labels specified in subdivision 3. The rules governing the requirement of labels specified in subdivision 3 may require other information relating to the public health and environmental effects of PCB and shall apply to persons holding certificates of exemption.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.37.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Penalties.</span></h2>
          <p>Violations of this section and sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.36">116.36</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116D.045">116D.045</a> shall be subject to the provisions of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115.071">115.071</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.37.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/344/#laws.0.2.0">1976 c 344 s 2</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116.38">
        <h1 class="shn">116.38 PCB BURNING.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.38.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">State policy.</span></h2>
          <p>The legislature finds that risks to human health must be adequately evaluated before a facility may burn PCBs. The legislature also finds that if there is a risk to human health, all human health must be treated with equal concern, and facilities that cause risks to human health must not be allowed to operate in sparsely populated areas if they would not be allowed to operate in heavily populated areas.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.38.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">EIS required.</span></h2>
          <p>The Pollution Control Agency may not allow burning of wastes containing 50 ppm or greater PCBs by permit or otherwise unless an environmental impact statement is completed. It may not renew a permit for burning wastes containing 50 ppm or greater PCBs until an environmental impact statement is completed. This section does not apply to experimental burning of small quantities of waste containing 50 ppm or greater PCBs.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.38.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/594/#laws.1.52.0">1990 c 594 art 1 s 52</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116.385">
        <h1 class="shn">116.385 TRICHLOROETHYLENE; BAN.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.385.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) For the purposes of this section, the following terms have the meanings given.</p>
          <p>(b) "Small business" means a business that has less than 500 full-time equivalent employees.</p>
          <p>(c) "Trichloroethylene" means a chemical with the Chemical Abstract Services Registry Number of 79-01-6.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.385.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Use restriction.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Beginning June 1, 2022, an owner or operator of a facility required to have an air emissions permit issued by the Pollution Control Agency may not use trichloroethylene at its permitted facility, including in any manufacturing, processing, or cleaning processes, except as otherwise provided in this section. Cessation of use must be made enforceable in the air emissions permit for the facility or in an enforceable agreement by June 1, 2022. The commissioner of the Pollution Control Agency must not issue an air emissions permit that authorizes using trichloroethylene at a permitted facility after January 1, 2022, except as described in paragraph (b) and subdivision 4.</p>
          <p>(b) If a small business needs additional time to assess replacement chemicals or modifications to facility operations, then by June 1, 2022, the commissioner shall include a schedule of compliance in the facility's permit or enter into an enforceable agreement that requires compliance with this section before June 1, 2023. A small business owner or operator requesting additional time under this paragraph must demonstrate compliance with the health-based value and health risk limits for trichloroethylene, as established by the Department of Health as of January 1, 2019. Owners or operators may be required to comply with additional restrictions based on impacts from nearby sources or background concentrations. Owners or operators may be required to provide additional information as requested by the commissioner to evaluate site-specific conditions or impacts.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.385.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Replacement chemicals.</span></h2>
          <p>An owner or operator that must comply with this section and elects to replace trichloroethylene with another chemical must replace trichloroethylene with a chemical demonstrated to be less toxic to human health and reviewed in a form determined and approved by the commissioner of the Pollution Control Agency.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.385.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Exceptions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner of the Pollution Control Agency shall grant exceptions to the prohibition in subdivision 2, for any of the following uses where compliance with the health-based value and health risk limits for trichloroethylene established by the Department of Health as of January 1, 2019, is demonstrated:</p>
          <p>(1) use of trichloroethylene in closed systems so that no trichloroethylene is emitted from the facility;</p>
          <p>(2) holding trichloroethylene or products containing trichloroethylene for distribution to a third party; and</p>
          <p>(3) a hospital licensed under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/144.50">144.50</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/144.56">144.56</a>, or an academic medical facility.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner of the Pollution Control Agency may grant exceptions to the prohibition in subdivision 2 through the variance process established in Minnesota Rules, part <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/7000.7000">7000.7000</a>, for any of the following uses where compliance with the health-based value and health risk limits for trichloroethylene established by the Department of Health as of January 1, 2019, is demonstrated:</p>
          <p>(1) a facility that uses trichloroethylene exclusively for research and development, or other laboratory or experimental purposes; and</p>
          <p>(2) a facility that processes trichloroethylene for waste disposal.</p>
          <p>(c) Owners or operators of facilities seeking an exception under this section must submit information to the commissioner that specifies the exception that applies and provide all information needed to determine applicability.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.385.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Application of exceptions.</span></h2>
          <p>Nothing in subdivision 4 shall be construed to authorize a use of an amount of trichloroethylene that exceeds the levels authorized in a stipulation agreement entered into between the Pollution Control Agency and a permittee that was in effect on June 1, 2022.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.385.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Short title.</span></h2>
          <p>This act is the "White Bear Area Neighborhood Concerned Citizens Group Ban TCE Act."</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.385.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2020/0/84/#laws.0.1.0">2020 c 84 s 1</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">OZONE LAYER; PRESERVATION</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116.39">
        <h1 class="shn">116.39 OZONE LAYER; PRESERVATION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.39.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Sale prohibited.</span></h2>
          <p>Except as provided by subdivision 3, after July 1, 1979, no person shall sell or offer for sale in this state any pressurized container which contains as a propellant trichloromonofluoromethane, difluorodichloromethane, dichlorotetrafluoroethane, or any other saturated chlorofluorocarbon compound or other similar inert fluorocarbon compound that does not contain reactive carbon hydrogen bonds.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.39.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Warning.</span></h2>
          <p>Commencing October 31, 1977, no person shall sell or offer for sale at wholesale in this state a pressurized container using chlorofluorocarbon propellants unless the container has prominently displayed on the front panel this statement: "Warning: Contains a chlorofluorocarbon that may harm the public health and environment by reducing ozone in the upper atmosphere."</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.39.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Other compounds permitted.</span></h2>
          <p>Nothing in this section prohibits the sale or use of refrigeration equipment containing chlorofluorocarbon compounds, or the sale of chlorofluorocarbon compounds for use in such equipment. This section shall not apply to the sale of chlorofluorocarbon compounds for the following essential medical uses:</p>
          <p>(1) metered-dose steroid human drugs for nasal inhalation;</p>
          <p>(2) metered-dose steroid human drugs for oral inhalation;</p>
          <p>(3) metered-dose adrenergic bronchodilator human drugs for oral inhalation;</p>
          <p>(4) contraceptive vaginal foams for human use; or</p>
          <p>(5) cytology fixatives; nor</p>
          <p>for other medical uses by or under the supervision of a licensed physician, dentist or veterinarian, or a hospital, nursing home or other health care institution licensed by the Department of Health. This section shall also not apply to the sale of chlorofluorocarbon compounds for use in the cleaning, maintenance, testing and repair of electronic equipment.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.39.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Penalty.</span></h2>
          <p>A violation of this section is a misdemeanor.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.39.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1977/0/373/#laws.0.1.0">1977 c 373 s 1</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116.391">
        <h1 class="shn">116.391 RESILIENT COMMUNITY ASSISTANCE PROGRAM.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.391.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Citation.</span></h2>
          <p>This section may be cited as the "Minnesota Resilient Community Act."</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.391.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) For purposes of this section, the terms defined in this subdivision have the meanings given.</p>
          <p>(b) "Commissioner" means the commissioner of the Pollution Control Agency.</p>
          <p>(c) "Local government unit" means any unit of government other than a state or federal unit of government and includes watershed districts established according to chapter 103D, soil and water conservation districts, watershed management organizations, counties, towns, cities, port authorities, housing authorities, regional development commissions, school districts, and the Metropolitan Council.</p>
          <p>(d) "Tribal government" means any of the Minnesota Tribal governments defined under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/10.65#stat.10.65.2">10.65, subdivision 2</a>, clause (4), and includes Tribal organizations designated by any of the Minnesota Tribal governments.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.391.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Establishment.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner must establish a resilient community assistance program to:</p>
          <p>(1) assist local government units, Tribal governments, and other relevant organizations as determined by the commissioner in adapting to and developing community resilience to impacts of climate change;</p>
          <p>(2) help coordinate climate adaptation planning, implementation, and evaluation efforts among state agencies, local government units, Tribal governments, and other relevant organizations; and</p>
          <p>(3) address inequities due to social, economic, historical, and political factors that result in some communities having less ability to prepare for, cope with, and recover from impacts of climate change.</p>
          <p>(b) To address inequities under paragraph (a), clause (3), the commissioner must seek input and collaboration from disproportionately impacted communities.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.391.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Program elements.</span></h2>
          <p>The resilient community assistance program may include but is not limited to:</p>
          <p>(1) developing, assembling, and disseminating information on climate adaptation and resilience;</p>
          <p>(2) technical assistance for climate adaptation and resilience;</p>
          <p>(3) financial assistance programs that provide grants or loans for resilience planning and for implementing climate adaptation and resilience actions, coordinated with the Public Facilities Authority, as necessary, for state bond-funded projects;</p>
          <p>(4) outreach, including seminars, workshops, training programs, and other similar activities, designed to provide education and information on climate adaptation and resilience to local government units, Tribal governments, and other relevant organizations as determined by the commissioner;</p>
          <p>(5) coordinating, implementing, and measuring progress on climate adaptation and resilience and measuring local government and Tribal government climate adaptation in Minnesota; and</p>
          <p>(6) other efforts needed to support climate adaptation and community resilience in Minnesota as determined by the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.391.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Administration.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) In administering the program, the commissioner may coordinate with administrators of other public and private programs that provide technical and financial assistance to local government units, Tribal governments, and other relevant organizations that receive assistance under this section.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner may make grants to or enter into contracts with public or private entities to operate elements of the program. Grantees under this paragraph must provide the commissioner with periodic reports on their efforts to assist in administering the program.</p>
          <p>(c) When operating or participating in elements of the program according to a grant or contract under paragraph (b), a person is an employee of the state who is certified to be acting within the scope of employment for purposes of indemnification under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/3.736">3.736</a>, subdivision 9, for claims that arise out of the information, assistance, and recommendations covered by the grant or contract. The state is not obligated to defend or indemnify a grantee or contractor under this subdivision to the extent of the grantee's or contractor's liability insurance. The grantee's or contractor's right to indemnity is not a waiver of limitations, defenses, and immunities available to either the grantee or contractor or the state by law.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.391.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Award for excellence in community resilience.</span></h2>
          <p>The governor or commissioner may issue annual awards in the form of a commendation for excellence in climate adaptation and resilience. The commissioner must administer applications for the awards.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.391.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/116/#laws.2.21.0">2024 c 116 art 2 s 21</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">WASTE FACILITY TRAINING AND CERTIFICATION</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116.41">
        <h1 class="shn">116.41 WASTE AND WASTE FACILITIES TRAINING AND CERTIFICATION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.41.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/373/#laws.0.72.0">1983 c 373 s 72</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.41.1a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1a.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/373/#laws.0.72.0">1983 c 373 s 72</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.41.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Training and certification programs.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The agency shall develop standards of competence for persons operating and inspecting various classes of disposal facilities. The agency shall conduct training programs for persons operating facilities for the disposal of waste and for inspectors of such facilities, and shall charge such fees as are necessary to cover the actual costs of the training programs. All fees received shall be paid into the state treasury and credited to the Pollution Control Agency training account and are appropriated to the agency to pay expenses relating to the training of disposal facility personnel.</p>
          <p>(b) The agency shall require operators and inspectors of such facilities to obtain from the agency a certificate of competence. The agency shall conduct examinations to test the competence of applicants for certification, and shall require that certificates be renewed at reasonable intervals. The agency may charge such fees as are necessary to cover the actual costs of receiving and processing applications, conducting examinations, and issuing and renewing certificates. Certificates shall not be required for a private individual for land-spreading and associated interim and temporary storage of sewage sludge on property owned or farmed by that individual.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.41.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Regulation and enforcement assistance.</span></h2>
          <p>The agency shall establish a program to provide technical and financial assistance for regulation and enforcement to counties which have certified operators and inspectors conforming to the requirements of the agency, chapters 400 and 473, and sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.01">115A.01</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.72">115A.72</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.41.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Rules.</span></h2>
          <p>The agency shall adopt, amend, and rescind rules as may be necessary to carry out the provisions of this section in accordance with chapter 14.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.41.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/646/#laws.0.1.0">1973 c 646 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/564/#laws.11.13.0">1980 c 564 art 11 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/352/#laws.0.29.0">1981 c 352 s 29</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/424/#laws.0.130.0">1982 c 424 s 130</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/301/#laws.0.118.0">1983 c 301 s 118</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/348/#laws.0.31.0">1987 c 348 s 31</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/404/#laws.0.146.0">1987 c 404 s 146</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/335/#laws.4.46.0">1989 c 335 art 4 s 46</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/37/#laws.1.46.0">2009 c 37 art 1 s 46</a></p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">TOXIC SUBSTANCES DEPOSITION</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116.42">
        <h1 class="shn">116.42 ACID DEPOSITION; LEGISLATIVE INTENT.</h1>
        <p>The legislature recognizes that acid deposition substantially resulting from the conduct of commercial and industrial operations, both within and without the state, poses a present and severe danger to the delicate balance of ecological systems within the state, and that the failure to act promptly and decisively to mitigate or eliminate this danger will soon result in untold and irreparable damage to the agricultural, water, forest, fish, and wildlife resources of the state. It is therefore the intent of the legislature in enacting sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.42">116.42</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.44">116.44</a> to mitigate or eliminate the acid deposition problem by curbing sources of acid deposition within the state and to support and encourage other states, the federal government, and the province of Ontario in recognizing the dangers of acid deposition and taking steps to mitigate or eliminate it within their own jurisdictions.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.42.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/482/#laws.0.1.0">1982 c 482 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/158/#laws.1.30.0">2016 c 158 art 1 s 30</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116.43">
        <h1 class="shn">116.43 ACID DEPOSITION DEFINED.</h1>
        <p>As used in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.42">116.42</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.44">116.44</a>, "acid deposition" means the wet or dry deposition from the atmosphere of chemical compounds, usually in the form of rain or snow, having the potential to form an aqueous compound with a pH level lower than the level considered normal under natural conditions, or lower than 5.6.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.43.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/482/#laws.0.2.0">1982 c 482 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/158/#laws.1.31.0">2016 c 158 art 1 s 31</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116.44">
        <h1 class="shn">116.44 SENSITIVE AREAS; STANDARDS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.44.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">List of areas.</span></h2>
          <p>By January 1, 1983, the Pollution Control Agency shall publish a preliminary list of counties determined to contain natural resources sensitive to the impacts of acid deposition. Sensitive areas shall be designated on the basis of:</p>
          <p>(1) the presence of plants and animal species which are sensitive to acid deposition;</p>
          <p>(2) geological information identifying those areas which have insoluble bedrock which is incapable of adequately neutralizing acid deposition; and</p>
          <p>(3) existing acid deposition reports and data prepared by the Pollution Control Agency and the federal Environmental Protection Agency. The Pollution Control Agency shall conduct public meetings on the preliminary list of acid deposition sensitive areas. Meetings shall be concluded by March 1, 1983, and a final list published by May 1, 1983.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.44.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Standards.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) By January 1, 1986, the agency shall adopt an acid deposition standard for wet plus dry acid deposition in the acid deposition sensitive areas listed pursuant to subdivision 1.</p>
          <p>(b) By January 1, 1986, the agency shall adopt an acid deposition control plan to attain and maintain the acid deposition standard adopted under clause (a), addressing sources both inside and outside of the state which emit more than 100 tons of sulphur dioxide per year. The plan shall include an analysis of the estimated compliance costs for facilities emitting sulphur dioxide. Any emission reductions required inside of the state shall be based on the contribution of sources inside of the state to acid deposition in excess of the standard.</p>
          <p>(c) By January 1, 1990, sources located inside the state shall be in compliance with the provisions of the acid deposition control plan.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.44.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/482/#laws.0.3.0">1982 c 482 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/519/#laws.0.1.0">1984 c 519 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/209/#laws.1.11.0">1989 c 209 art 1 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/187/#laws.1.11.0">1997 c 187 art 1 s 11</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116.45"><b>116.45</b> [Obsolete, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.2.161.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 2 s 161</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116.454">
        <h1 class="shn">116.454 MONITORING PROGRAM.</h1>
        <p>By July 1, 1993, the agency shall establish a statewide monitoring program for, and inventory of probable sources of, releases into the air, ambient concentrations in the air, and deposition from the air of toxic substances.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.454.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/546/#laws.0.3.0">1992 c 546 s 3</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">STORAGE TANKS</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116.46">
        <h1 class="shn">116.46 DEFINITIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.46.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Scope.</span></h2>
          <p>As used in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.47">116.47</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.50">116.50</a>, the terms defined in this section have the meanings given them.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.46.1a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1a.<span class="headnote">Aboveground storage tank.</span></h2>
          <p>"Aboveground storage tank" means any one or a combination of containers, vessels, and enclosures, including structures and appurtenances connected to them, that is used to contain or dispense regulated substances, and that is not an underground storage tank.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.46.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Agency.</span></h2>
          <p>"Agency" means the Pollution Control Agency.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.46.2a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2a.<span class="headnote">Installer.</span></h2>
          <p>"Installer" means a person who places, constructs, or repairs an aboveground or underground tank, or permanently takes an aboveground or underground tank out of service.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.46.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Operator.</span></h2>
          <p>"Operator" means a person in control of, or having responsibility for, the daily operation of an underground storage tank.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.46.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Owner.</span></h2>
          <p>"Owner" means a person who owns an underground storage tank and a person who owned it immediately before discontinuation of its use.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.46.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Person.</span></h2>
          <p>"Person" has the meaning given it in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.06#stat.116.06.17">116.06, subdivision 17</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.46.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Regulated substance.</span></h2>
          <p>"Regulated substance" means:</p>
          <p>(1) a hazardous material listed in Code of Federal Regulations, title 49, section 172.101; or</p>
          <p>(2) petroleum, including crude oil or a fraction of crude oil that is liquid at a temperature of 60 degrees Fahrenheit and pressure of 14.7 pounds per square inch absolute.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.46.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Release.</span></h2>
          <p>"Release" means a spilling, leaking, emitting, discharging, escaping, leaching, or disposing from an underground storage tank into the environment. Release does not include designed venting consistent with the agency's air quality rules.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.46.7a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7a.<span class="headnote">Retail location.</span></h2>
          <p>"Retail location" means a facility located in the metropolitan area as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/473.121#stat.473.121.2">473.121, subdivision 2</a>, where gasoline is offered for sale to the general public for use in automobiles and trucks.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.46.7b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7b.<span class="headnote">Transport delivery vehicle.</span></h2>
          <p>"Transport delivery vehicle" means a liquid fuel cargo tank used to deliver gasoline into underground storage tanks.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.46.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Underground storage tank.</span></h2>
          <p>"Underground storage tank" means any one or a combination of containers including tanks, vessels, enclosures, or structures and underground appurtenances connected to them, that is used to contain or dispense an accumulation of regulated substances and the volume of which, including the volume of the underground pipes connected to them, is ten percent or more beneath the surface of the ground.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.46.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.</h2>
          <p>[Renumbered subd 1a]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.46.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Vapor recovery system.</span></h2>
          <p>"Vapor recovery system" means a system which transfers vapors from underground storage tanks during the filling operation to the storage compartment of the transport vehicle delivering gasoline.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.46.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/1/13/#laws.0.235.0">1Sp1985 c 13 s 235</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/389/#laws.0.11.0">1987 c 389 s 11</a>,12; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/128/#laws.1.140.0">2003 c 128 art 1 s 140</a>-142</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116.47">
        <h1 class="shn">116.47 EXEMPTIONS.</h1>
        <p>Sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.48">116.48</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.49">116.49</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.491">116.491</a> do not apply to:</p>
        <p>(1) farm or residential tanks of 1,100 gallons or less capacity used for storing motor fuel for noncommercial purposes;</p>
        <p>(2) tanks of 1,100 gallons or less capacity used for storing heating oil for consumptive use on the premises where stored;</p>
        <p>(3) pipeline facilities, including gathering lines, regulated under the Natural Gas Pipeline Safety Act of 1968, United States Code, title 49, chapter 24, or the Hazardous Liquid Pipeline Safety Act of 1979, United States Code, title 49, chapter 29;</p>
        <p>(4) surface impoundments, pits, ponds, or lagoons;</p>
        <p>(5) stormwater or wastewater collection systems;</p>
        <p>(6) flow-through process tanks;</p>
        <p>(7) tanks located in an underground area, including basements, cellars, mine workings, drifts, shafts, or tunnels, if the storage tank is located upon or above the surface of the floor;</p>
        <p>(8) septic tanks;</p>
        <p>(9) tanks used for storing liquids that are gaseous at atmospheric temperature and pressure; or</p>
        <p>(10) tanks used for storing agricultural chemicals regulated under chapter 18B, 18C, or 18D.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.47.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/1/13/#laws.0.236.0">1Sp1985 c 13 s 236</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/389/#laws.0.13.0">1987 c 389 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/87/#laws.0.2.0">1993 c 87 s 2</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116.48">
        <h1 class="shn">116.48 NOTIFICATION REQUIREMENTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.48.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Tank status.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) An owner of an underground storage tank must notify the agency by June 1, 1986, or within 30 days after installation, whichever is later, of the tank's existence and specify the age, size, type, location, uses, and contents of the tank on forms prescribed by the agency.</p>
          <p>(b) An owner of an aboveground storage tank must notify the agency by June 1, 1990, or within 30 days after installation, whichever is later, of the tank's existence and specify the age, size, type, location, uses, and contents of the tank on forms prescribed by the agency.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.48.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Abandoned tanks.</span></h2>
          <p>An owner of an underground or aboveground storage tank permanently taken out of service on or after January 1, 1974, must notify the agency by June 1, 1986, in the case of underground storage tanks; by June 1, 1990, in the case of aboveground storage tanks; or, in either case, within 30 days of discovery, whichever is later, of the existence of the tank and specify or estimate to the best of the owner's knowledge on forms prescribed by the agency, the date the tank was taken out of service, the age, size, type, and location of the tank, and the type and quantity of substance remaining in the tank.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.48.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Change in status.</span></h2>
          <p>An owner must notify the agency within 30 days of a permanent removal from service or a change in the reported uses, contents, or ownership of an underground or aboveground storage tank.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.48.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Deposit information.</span></h2>
          <p>Beginning on January 1, 1986, and until July 1, 1987, a person who transfers the title to regulated substances to be placed directly into an underground storage tank must inform the owner or operator in writing of the notification requirement of this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.48.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Seller's responsibility.</span></h2>
          <p>A person who sells a tank intended to be used as an underground or aboveground storage tank or property that the seller knows contains an underground or aboveground storage tank must inform the purchaser in writing of the owner's notification requirements of this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.48.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Affidavit.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Before transferring ownership of property that the owner knows contains an underground or aboveground storage tank or contained an underground or aboveground storage tank that had a release for which no corrective action was taken or if required by the agency as a condition of a corrective action under chapter 115C, the owner shall record with the county recorder or registrar of titles of the county in which the property is located an affidavit containing:</p>
          <p>(1) a legal description of the property where the tank is located;</p>
          <p>(2) a description of the tank, of the location of the tank, and of any known release from the tank of a regulated substance to the full extent known or reasonably ascertainable;</p>
          <p>(3) a description of any restrictions currently in force on the use of the property resulting from any release; and</p>
          <p>(4) the name of the owner.</p>
          <p>(b) The county recorder shall record the affidavits in a manner that will insure their disclosure in the ordinary course of a title search of the subject property. Before transferring ownership of property that the owner knows contains an underground or aboveground storage tank, the owner shall deliver to the purchaser a copy of the affidavit and any additional information necessary to make the facts in the affidavit accurate as of the date of transfer of ownership.</p>
          <p>(c) Failure to record an affidavit as provided in this subdivision does not affect or prevent any transfer of ownership of the property.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.48.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Recording removal affidavit.</span></h2>
          <p>If an affidavit has been recorded under subdivision 6 and the tank and any regulated substance released from the tank have been removed from the property in accordance with applicable law, the owner or other interested party may file with the county recorder or registrar of titles an affidavit stating the name of the owner, the legal description of the property, the place and date of filing and document number of the affidavit filed under subdivision 6, and the approximate date of removal of the tank and regulated substance. Upon filing the affidavit described in this subdivision, the affidavit and the affidavit filed under subdivision 6, together with the information set forth in the affidavits, cease to constitute either actual or constructive notice.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.48.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Notice of tank installation or removal.</span></h2>
          <p>Before beginning installation or removal of an underground tank system, owners and operators must notify the commissioner. Notification must be in writing or by telephone at least ten days before the tank installation or removal. Owners and operators must renotify the commissioner if the date of the tank installation or removal changes by more than 48 hours. The notification must include the following information:</p>
          <p>(1) the name, address, and telephone number of the site owner;</p>
          <p>(2) the location of the site, if different from clause (1);</p>
          <p>(3) the date of the tank installation or removal; and</p>
          <p>(4) the name of the contractor or company that will install or remove the tank.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.48.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/1/13/#laws.0.237.0">1Sp1985 c 13 s 237</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/389/#laws.0.14.0">1987 c 389 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/686/#laws.1.60.0">1988 c 686 art 1 s 60</a>,61; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/226/#laws.0.4.0">1989 c 226 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/490/#laws.0.11.0">1992 c 490 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/114/#laws.4.87.0">2013 c 114 art 4 s 87</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116.481">
        <h1 class="shn">116.481 MONITORING.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.481.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Measuring tank capacity.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) By September 1, 1996, all aboveground tanks of 2,000 gallons or more used for storage and subsequent resale of petroleum products must be equipped with:</p>
          <p>(1) a gauge in working order that shows the current level of product in the tank; or</p>
          <p>(2) an audible or visual alarm which alerts the person delivering fuel into the tank that the tank is within 100 gallons of capacity.</p>
          <p>(b) In lieu of the equipment specified in paragraph (a), the owner or operator of a tank may use a manual method of measurement which accurately determines the amount of product in the tank and the amount of capacity available to be used. This information must be readily available to anyone delivering fuel into the tank prior to delivery. Documentation that a tank has the available capacity for the amount of product to be delivered must be transmitted to the person making the delivery.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.481.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Contents labeled.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) By December 1, 1995, all aboveground tanks governed by this section must be numbered and labeled as to the tank contents, total capacity, and capacity in volume increments of 500 gallons or less.</p>
          <p>(b) Piping connected to the tank must be labeled with the product carried at the point of delivery and at the tank inlet. Manifolded delivery points must have all valves labeled as to product distribution.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.481.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Site diagram.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) All tanks at a facility shall be shown on a site diagram which is permanently mounted in an area accessible to delivery personnel. The diagram shall show the number, capacity, and contents of tanks and the location of piping, valves, storm sewers, and other information necessary for emergency response, including the facility owner's or operator's telephone number.</p>
          <p>(b) Prior to delivering product into an underground or aboveground tank, delivery personnel shall:</p>
          <p>(1) consult the site diagram, where applicable, for proper delivery points, tank and piping locations, and valve settings;</p>
          <p>(2) visually inspect the tank, piping, and valve settings to determine that the product being delivered will flow only into the appropriate tank; and</p>
          <p>(3) determine, using equipment and information available at the site, that the available capacity of the tank is sufficient to hold the amount being delivered.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) Delivery personnel must remain in attendance during delivery.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.481.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Capacity of tank.</span></h2>
          <p>A tank may not be filled from a transport vehicle compartment containing more than the available capacity of the tank, unless the hose of the transport vehicle is equipped with a manually operated shutoff nozzle.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.481.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Exemption.</span></h2>
          <p>Aboveground and underground tanks located at refineries, pipeline terminals, and river terminals are exempt from this section.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.481.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/240/#laws.1.13.0">1995 c 240 art 1 s 13</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116.482">
        <h1 class="shn">116.482 PETROLEUM RELEASE; NOTIFICATION.</h1>
        <p class="in">(a) When a potential receptor survey is conducted for a petroleum tank release as provided in agency guidance documents, the tank owner must provide information on the results of the survey, reports of all releases, and any corrective actions, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115C.02">115C.02</a>, that are related to the petroleum tank release in an understandable manner to residents contacted in the survey. The information may be provided through personal contact, mail, or email.</p>
        <p class="in">(b) An owner may delegate the owner's responsibility under paragraph (a) to the owner's consultant or contractor, as those terms are defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115C.02">115C.02</a>, or to the operator of the tank.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.482.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/357/#laws.0.35.0">2008 c 357 s 35</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116.49">
        <h1 class="shn">116.49 ENVIRONMENTAL PROTECTION REQUIREMENTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.49.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Rules.</span></h2>
          <p>The agency must adopt rules applicable to all owners and operators of underground storage tanks. The rules must establish the safeguards necessary to protect human health and the environment. The agency may delay adopting the rules until the United States Environmental Protection Agency proposes regulations for regulated substances, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.46#stat.116.46.6">116.46, subdivision 6</a>, clause (1). The agency shall delay adopting the rules for regulated substances, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.46#stat.116.46.6">116.46, subdivision 6</a>, clause (2), until the United States Environmental Protection Agency publishes final regulations for underground storage tanks, or February 8, 1987, whichever is earlier.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.49.1a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1a.<span class="headnote">Tank on tax-forfeited land.</span></h2>
          <p>The state, an agency of the state, or a political subdivision is not considered an owner or operator of a tank solely as a result of the forfeiture of title to the tank or real property where the tank is located for nonpayment of taxes, or solely as a result of actions taken to manage, sell, or transfer tax-forfeited land where a tank is located under chapter 282 and other laws applicable to tax-forfeited lands. This subdivision does not relieve the state, a state agency, or a political subdivision from liability for the daily operation of a tank under its control or responsibility located on tax-forfeited land.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.49.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Interim standards.</span></h2>
          <p>Until the rules required by subdivision 1 become effective, a person may not install an underground storage tank unless the tank:</p>
          <p>(1) is installed according to requirements of the American Petroleum Institute Bulletin 1615 (November 1979) and all manufacturer's recommendations;</p>
          <p>(2) is cathodically protected against corrosion, constructed of noncorrosive material, steel clad with a noncorrosive material, or designed in a manner to prevent the release of any stored substance; and</p>
          <p>(3) is constructed to be compatible with the substance to be stored.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.49.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Vapor recovery system.</span></h2>
          <p>Every underground gasoline storage tank at a retail location must be fitted with vapor recovery equipment by January 1, 2006. The equipment must be certified by the manufacturer as capable of collecting 95 percent of hydrocarbons emitted during gasoline transfers from a transport delivery vehicle to an underground storage tank. Product delivery and vapor recovery access points must be on the same side of the transport vehicle when the transport vehicle is positioned for delivery into the underground tank. After January 1, 2006, no gasoline may be delivered to a retail location that is not equipped with a vapor recovery system.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.49.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Vapor recovery on transports.</span></h2>
          <p>All transport delivery vehicles that deliver gasoline into underground storage tanks in the metropolitan area as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/473.121#stat.473.121.2">473.121, subdivision 2</a>, must be fitted with vapor recovery equipment. The equipment must recover and manage 95 percent of hydrocarbons emitted during the transfer of gasoline from the underground storage tank and the transport delivery vehicle by January 1, 2006. After January 1, 2006, no gasoline may be delivered to a retail location by a transport vehicle that is not fitted with vapor recovery equipment.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.49.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/1/13/#laws.0.238.0">1Sp1985 c 13 s 238</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/586/#laws.0.5.0">1990 c 586 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/128/#laws.1.143.0">2003 c 128 art 1 s 143</a>,144</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116.491">
        <h1 class="shn">116.491 TANK INSTALLERS; TRAINING AND CERTIFICATION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.491.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Requirement.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) After the effective date of rules adopted under subdivision 3, a person may not install, repair, or take an aboveground or underground tank permanently out of service without first obtaining a certification of competence issued by the agency.</p>
          <p>(b) The agency shall conduct examinations to test the competence of applicants for certification, issue documentation of certification, and require certification to be renewed at reasonable intervals. The agency may conduct training programs for installers.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.491.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Fees.</span></h2>
          <p>The agency may charge fees as are necessary to cover the actual costs of processing applications, conducting examinations, issuing and renewing certificates, and providing training programs. The fees received under this section must be credited to the petroleum tank release cleanup fund.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.491.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Rules.</span></h2>
          <p>The agency shall adopt rules containing standards of competence for installers and to implement this section.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.491.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/389/#laws.0.15.0">1987 c 389 s 15</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116.492">
        <h1 class="shn">116.492 BASEMENT STORAGE TANKS; REMOVAL.</h1>
        <p>A person who removes a basement heating oil storage tank shall ensure that fill and vent pipes through the basement wall to the outside are also removed or permanently sealed.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.492.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/597/#laws.0.3.0">1992 c 597 s 3</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116.50">
        <h1 class="shn">116.50 PREEMPTION.</h1>
        <p>Sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.46">116.46</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.49">116.49</a> preempt conflicting local and municipal rules or ordinances requiring notification or establishing environmental protection requirements for underground storage tanks. A state agency or local unit of government may not adopt rules or ordinances establishing or requiring vapor recovery for underground storage tanks.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.50.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/1/13/#laws.0.239.0">1Sp1985 c 13 s 239</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/128/#laws.1.145.0">2003 c 128 art 1 s 145</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116.51"><b>116.51</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/522/#laws.0.48.0">1992 c 522 s 48</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/595/#laws.0.29.0">1992 c 595 s 29</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116.52"><b>116.52</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/522/#laws.0.48.0">1992 c 522 s 48</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/595/#laws.0.29.0">1992 c 595 s 29</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr_by_subd" id="stat.116.53">
        <h1>116.53 </h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.53.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/522/#laws.0.48.0">1992 c 522 s 48</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/595/#laws.0.29.0">1992 c 595 s 29</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.53.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p> [Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/144.878">144.878, subd 2a</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116.54"><b>116.54</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/248/#laws.0.19.0">2014 c 248 s 19</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116.55"><b>116.55</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/685/#laws.0.44.0">1988 c 685 s 44</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116.60"><b>116.60</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/178/#laws.0.10.0">1999 c 178 s 10</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116.61"><b>116.61</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/178/#laws.0.10.0">1999 c 178 s 10</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116.62"><b>116.62</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/178/#laws.0.10.0">1999 c 178 s 10</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116.63"><b>116.63</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/178/#laws.0.10.0">1999 c 178 s 10</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116.64"><b>116.64</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/178/#laws.0.10.0">1999 c 178 s 10</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116.65"><b>116.65</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/178/#laws.0.10.0">1999 c 178 s 10</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116.66"><b>116.66</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/247/#laws.1.41.0">1995 c 247 art 1 s 41</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116.67"><b>116.67</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/2/#laws.0.162.0">1Sp2001 c 2 s 162</a>]</div>
      <h1 class="fgh">CHLOROFLUOROCARBON REGULATION</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116.70">
        <h1 class="shn">116.70 DEFINITIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.70.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Applicability.</span></h2>
          <p>The definitions in this section apply to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.731">116.731</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.734">116.734</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.70.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/2/#laws.0.162.0">1Sp2001 c 2 s 162</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.70.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Chlorofluorocarbons or CFCs.</span></h2>
          <p>"Chlorofluorocarbons" or "CFCs" means the substances identified as class I or class II substances under section 602 of the Clean Air Act, United States Code, title 42, section 7401 et seq., as amended by the Clean Air Act Amendments of 1990, Public Law 101-549.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.70.3a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3a.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/2/#laws.0.162.0">1Sp2001 c 2 s 162</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.70.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/2/#laws.0.162.0">1Sp2001 c 2 s 162</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.70.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.</h2>
          <p>[Renumbered subd 3a]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.70.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/671/#laws.0.1.0">1988 c 671 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/560/#laws.2.3.0">1990 c 560 art 2 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/546/#laws.0.4.0">1992 c 546 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/2/#laws.0.138.0">1Sp2001 c 2 s 138</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116.71"><b>116.71</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/2/#laws.0.162.0">1Sp2001 c 2 s 162</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116.72"><b>116.72</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/2/#laws.0.162.0">1Sp2001 c 2 s 162</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116.73"><b>116.73</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/2/#laws.0.162.0">1Sp2001 c 2 s 162</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116.731">
        <h1 class="shn">116.731 REQUIREMENTS TO RECYCLE CFC'S.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.731.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Salvage automobiles.</span></h2>
          <p>A person who processes automobiles for salvage must remove CFCs for recycling prior to disposal or sale of the materials containing CFCs. This subdivision does not apply to crushed automobiles or automobiles that have been processed in a manner that makes removal and recovery of CFCs impossible.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.731.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Refrigeration equipment.</span></h2>
          <p>A person processing scrap refrigerators, air conditioning units, dehumidifiers, heat pumps, under-the-counter ice makers, vending machines, drinking water coolers, chillers, commercial refrigeration, industrial process refrigeration, or freezers must remove and recycle, destroy, or properly dispose of the CFCs.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.731.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Mobile air conditioning equipment.</span></h2>
          <p>A person servicing or removing mobile air conditioning equipment must:</p>
          <p>(1) recapture CFCs, provide storage for recaptured CFCs, and transfer recaptured CFCs to a recycler; or</p>
          <p>(2) recapture CFCs and recycle the CFCs to an allowed use.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.731.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Servicing and recycling appliances.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A person servicing or recycling refrigerators, air conditioning units, dehumidifiers, heat pumps, under-the-counter ice makers, vending machines, drinking water coolers, chillers, commercial refrigeration, industrial process refrigeration, or freezers must:</p>
