          <p>(1) recapture CFCs, provide storage for recaptured CFCs, and transfer recaptured CFCs to a recycler; or</p>
          <p>(2) recapture CFCs and recycle the CFCs to an allowed use.</p>
          <p>(b) The recovered CFCs may be properly disposed of or destroyed.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.731.4a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4a.<span class="headnote">Venting.</span></h2>
          <p>A person may not knowingly vent or otherwise release into the environment any CFC used as a refrigerant.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.731.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Foam not required to be recycled.</span></h2>
          <p>This section does not require recycling of rigid or flexible foam.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.731.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Rules.</span></h2>
          <p>The agency shall adopt rules for recycling CFCs and establish standards for CFC recycling equipment under this section.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.731.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/560/#laws.2.4.0">1990 c 560 art 2 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/585/#laws.0.34.0">1994 c 585 s 34</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/147/#laws.0.1.0">1995 c 147 s 1</a>-3</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116.732">
        <h1 class="shn">116.732 REQUIREMENT TO RECYCLE FIRE EXTINGUISHER HALONS.</h1>
        <p>A person who recharges, services, or retires fire extinguishers must recapture and recycle halons.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.732.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/560/#laws.2.5.0">1990 c 560 art 2 s 5</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116.733">
        <h1 class="shn">116.733 MEDICAL DEVICE EXEMPTION.</h1>
        <p>Laws 1990, chapter 560, article 2, sections 1 and 2, and sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.70">116.70</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.731">116.731</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.732">116.732</a>, do not apply to processes using CFCs or halons on medical devices, in sterilization processes in health care facilities, or by a person or facility in manufacturing or selling of medical devices.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.733.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/560/#laws.2.6.0">1990 c 560 art 2 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/199/#laws.1.30.0">1991 c 199 art 1 s 30</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116.734">
        <h1 class="shn">116.734 UNIFORM CFC REGULATION.</h1>
        <p>It is the policy of this state to regulate and manage CFCs in a uniform manner throughout the state. Political subdivisions may not adopt, and are preempted from adopting or enforcing, requirements relating to CFCs that are different than state law.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.734.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/560/#laws.2.7.0">1990 c 560 art 2 s 7</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116.735">
        <h1 class="shn">116.735 TRAINING AND CERTIFICATION.</h1>
        <p>(a) The agency shall develop standards of competence for persons who engage in activities relating to products that may contain CFCs, as described in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.731">116.731</a>, subdivisions 1 to 4, and the commissioner may conduct training programs for these persons. The persons shall obtain from the commissioner a certificate of competence or equivalent federal certification that has been approved by the commissioner.</p>
        <p>(b) The agency may adopt rules to implement this section.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.735.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/585/#laws.0.35.0">1994 c 585 s 35</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/147/#laws.0.4.0">1995 c 147 s 4</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116.74"><b>116.74</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/2/#laws.0.162.0">1Sp2001 c 2 s 162</a>]</div>
      <h1 class="fgh">INFECTIOUS WASTE CONTROL ACT</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116.75">
        <h1 class="shn">116.75 CITATION.</h1>
        <p>Sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.76">116.76</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.82">116.82</a> may be cited as the "Infectious Waste Control Act."</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.75.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/337/#laws.0.1.0">1989 c 337 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/206/#laws.0.2.0">1993 c 206 s 2</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116.76">
        <h1 class="shn">116.76 DEFINITIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.76.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Applicability.</span></h2>
          <p>The definitions in this section apply to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.76">116.76</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.83">116.83</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.76.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Agency.</span></h2>
          <p>"Agency" means the Pollution Control Agency.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.76.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Blood.</span></h2>
          <p>"Blood" means waste human blood and blood products in containers, or solid waste saturated and dripping human blood or blood products. Human blood products include serum, plasma, and other blood components.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.76.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Commercial transporter.</span></h2>
          <p>"Commercial transporter" means a person, other than the United States government, who transports infectious or pathological waste for compensation.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.76.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Commissioner.</span></h2>
          <p>"Commissioner" means the commissioner of the Pollution Control Agency.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.76.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Decontamination.</span></h2>
          <p>"Decontamination" means rendering infectious waste safe for routine handling as a solid waste.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.76.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/1/1/#laws.9.75.0">1Sp1993 c 1 art 9 s 75</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.76.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Facility.</span></h2>
          <p>"Facility" means a site where infectious waste is generated, stored, decontaminated, incinerated, or disposed.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.76.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Generator.</span></h2>
          <p>"Generator" means a person whose activities produce infectious waste. "Generator" does not include a person who produces sharps as a result of administering medication to oneself. "Generator" does not include an ambulance service licensed under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/144E.10">144E.10</a>, an eligible community health board, or public health nursing agency as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.78#stat.116.78.10">116.78, subdivision 10</a>, or a program providing school health service under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/121A.21">121A.21</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.76.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Household.</span></h2>
          <p>"Household" means a single detached dwelling unit or a single unit of a multiple dwelling.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.76.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.<span class="headnote">Infectious agent.</span></h2>
          <p>"Infectious agent" means an organism that is capable of producing infection or infectious disease in humans.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.76.12">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 12.<span class="headnote">Infectious waste.</span></h2>
          <p>"Infectious waste" means laboratory waste, blood, regulated body fluids, sharps, and research animal waste that have not been decontaminated.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.76.13">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 13.<span class="headnote">Laboratory waste.</span></h2>
          <p>"Laboratory waste" means waste cultures and stocks of agents that are generated from a laboratory and are infectious to humans; discarded contaminated items used to inoculate, transfer, or otherwise manipulate cultures or stocks of agents that are infectious to humans; wastes from the production of biological agents that are infectious to humans; and discarded live or attenuated vaccines that are infectious to humans.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.76.14">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 14.<span class="headnote">Pathological waste.</span></h2>
          <p>"Pathological waste" means human tissues and body parts removed accidentally or during surgery or autopsy intended for disposal.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.76.15">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 15.<span class="headnote">Person.</span></h2>
          <p>"Person" means an individual, partnership, association, public or private corporation, or other legal entity, the United States government, an interstate body, the state, and an agency, department, or political subdivision of the state.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.76.16">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 16.<span class="headnote">Regulated human body fluids.</span></h2>
          <p>"Regulated human body fluids" means cerebrospinal fluid, synovial fluid, pleural fluid, peritoneal fluid, pericardial fluid, and amniotic fluid that are in containers or that drip freely from body fluid soaked solid waste items.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.76.17">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 17.<span class="headnote">Research animal waste.</span></h2>
          <p>"Research animal waste" means carcasses, body parts, and blood derived from animals knowingly and intentionally exposed to agents that are infectious to humans for the purpose of research, production of biologicals, or testing of pharmaceuticals.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.76.18">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 18.<span class="headnote">Sharps.</span></h2>
          <p>"Sharps" means:</p>
          <p>(1) discarded items that can induce subdermal inoculation of infectious agents, including needles, lancets, scalpel blades, pipettes, and other items derived from human or animal patient care, blood banks, laboratories, mortuaries, research facilities, and industrial operations; and</p>
          <p>(2) discarded glass or rigid plastic vials containing infectious agents.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.76.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/337/#laws.0.2.0">1989 c 337 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/568/#laws.2.2.0">1990 c 568 art 2 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/206/#laws.0.3.0">1993 c 206 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/1/1/#laws.9.2.0">1Sp1993 c 1 art 9 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/1/6/#laws.0.3.0">1Sp1993 c 6 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/585/#laws.0.36.0">1994 c 585 s 36</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/199/#laws.0.14.0">1997 c 199 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.11.3.0">1998 c 397 art 11 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/286/#laws.0.1.0">2010 c 286 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/0/21/#laws.1.109.0">2015 c 21 art 1 s 109</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116.77">
        <h1 class="shn">116.77 COVERAGE.</h1>
        <p>Sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.75">116.75</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.83">116.83</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.671#stat.609.671.11">609.671, subdivision 11</a>, cover any person, including a veterinarian, who generates, treats, stores, transports, or disposes of infectious or pathological waste but not including infectious or pathological waste generated by households, farm operations, or agricultural businesses. Except as specifically provided, sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.75">116.75</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.83">116.83</a> do not limit or alter treatment or disposal methods for infectious or pathological waste.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.77.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/337/#laws.0.3.0">1989 c 337 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/344/#laws.0.1.0">1991 c 344 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/206/#laws.0.4.0">1993 c 206 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/1/6/#laws.0.4.0">1Sp1993 c 6 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/158/#laws.1.32.0">2016 c 158 art 1 s 32</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116.78">
        <h1 class="shn">116.78 WASTE MANAGEMENT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.78.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Segregation.</span></h2>
          <p>All untreated infectious waste must be segregated from other waste material at its point of generation and maintained in separate packaging throughout collection, storage, and transport. Infectious waste must be packaged, contained, and transported in a manner that prevents release of the waste material.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.78.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Labeling.</span></h2>
          <p>All bags, boxes, and other containers used to collect, transport, or store infectious waste must be clearly labeled with a biohazard symbol or with the words "infectious waste" written in letters no less than one inch in height.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.78.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Reusable containers.</span></h2>
          <p>Containers which have been in direct contact with infectious waste must be disinfected prior to reuse.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.78.3a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3a.<span class="headnote">Waste containers.</span></h2>
          <p>Noninfectious mixed municipal solid waste generated by a facility must be placed for containment, collection, and processing or disposal in containers that are sufficiently transparent that the contents of the containers may be viewed from the exterior of the containers. The operator of a mixed municipal solid waste facility may not refuse to accept mixed municipal solid waste generated by a facility that complies with this subdivision, unless the operator observes that the waste contains sharps or other infectious waste.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.78.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Sharps.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A person shall not place sharps with recyclable materials, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.03">115A.03</a>.</p>
          <p>(b) Sharps, except those generated from a household or from a farm operation or agricultural business:</p>
          <p>(1) must be placed in puncture-resistant containers;</p>
          <p>(2) may not be compacted or mixed with other waste material whether or not the sharps are decontaminated unless it is part of an infectious waste decontamination process approved by the commissioner of the Pollution Control Agency that will prevent exposure during transportation and disposal; and</p>
          <p>(3) may not be disposed of at refuse-derived fuel facilities or at other facilities where waste is hand sorted.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.78.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Pathological waste.</span></h2>
          <p>Pathological waste must be managed according to sanitary standards established by state and federal laws or regulations for the disposal of the waste.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.78.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Storage.</span></h2>
          <p>Infectious and pathological waste must be stored in a specially designated area that is designed to prevent the entry of vermin and that prevents access by unauthorized persons.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.78.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Compacting and mixing with other wastes.</span></h2>
          <p>Infectious waste may not be compacted or mixed with other waste materials prior to incineration or disposal. Compaction is acceptable if it is part of an infectious waste system, approved by the commissioner of the Pollution Control Agency, that is designed to prevent exposure during storage, transportation, and disposal.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.78.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Disposal.</span></h2>
          <p>Except for disposal procedures specifically prescribed, this section and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.81">116.81</a> do not limit disposal methods for infectious and pathological waste.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.78.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Disposal of infectious waste by ambulance services.</span></h2>
          <p>Any infectious waste, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.76#stat.116.76.12">116.76, subdivision 12</a>, produced by an ambulance service in the transport or care of a patient must be properly packaged and disposed of at the destination hospital or at the nearest hospital if the patient is not transported. A hospital must accept the infectious waste if it is properly packaged according to the standards the hospital uses for packaging its own infectious wastes. The hospital may charge the ambulance service a reasonable fee for disposal of the infectious waste. Nothing in this subdivision shall require a hospital to accept infectious waste if the waste is of a type not generated by the hospital or if the hospital cannot safely store the waste. A hospital that accepts infectious waste under this subdivision is not subject to those provisions of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.79#stat.116.79.4">116.79, subdivision 4</a>, paragraph (a), that apply to the storage or decontamination of infectious or pathological waste generated at a site other than the hospital.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.78.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Disposal of infectious waste by public health agencies and programs providing school health services.</span></h2>
          <p>Any infectious waste, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.76#stat.116.76.12">116.76, subdivision 12</a>, produced by an eligible community health board, or public health nursing agency, or a program providing school health services under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/121A.21">121A.21</a>, must be properly packaged and may be disposed of at a hospital. For purposes of this subdivision, an "eligible community health board or public health nursing agency" is defined as a community health board or public health nursing agency located in a county with a population of less than 40,000. A hospital must accept the infectious waste if it is properly packaged according to the standards the hospital uses for packaging its own infectious wastes. The hospital may charge an eligible community health board or public health nursing agency or a program providing school health services a reasonable fee for disposal of the infectious waste. Nothing in this subdivision shall require a hospital to accept infectious waste if the waste is of a type not generated by the hospital or if the hospital cannot safely store the waste. A hospital that accepts infectious waste under this subdivision is not subject to those provisions of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.79#stat.116.79.4">116.79, subdivision 4</a>, paragraph (a), that apply to the storage or decontamination of infectious or pathological waste generated at a site other than the hospital.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.78.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/337/#laws.0.4.0">1989 c 337 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/568/#laws.2.3.0">1990 c 568 art 2 s 3</a>,4; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/344/#laws.0.2.0">1991 c 344 s 2</a>,3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/249/#laws.0.27.0">1993 c 249 s 27</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/1/1/#laws.9.3.0">1Sp1993 c 1 art 9 s 3</a>,4; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.11.3.0">1998 c 397 art 11 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/225/#laws.0.5.0">2014 c 225 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/0/21/#laws.1.109.0">2015 c 21 art 1 s 109</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116.79">
        <h1 class="shn">116.79 MANAGEMENT PLANS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.79.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Preparing management plans.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) To the extent applicable to the facility, a person in charge of a facility that generates, stores, decontaminates, incinerates, or disposes of infectious or pathological waste must prepare a management plan for the infectious or pathological waste handled by the facility. A person may prepare a common management plan for all generating facilities owned and operated by the person. If a single plan is prepared to cover multiple facilities, the plan must identify common policy and procedures for the facilities and any management procedures that are facility specific. The plan must identify each generating facility covered by the plan. A management plan must list all physicians, dentists, chiropractors, podiatrists, veterinarians, certified nurse practitioners, certified nurse midwives, or physician assistants, employed by, under contract to, or working at the generating facilities, except hospitals or laboratories. A management plan from a hospital must list the number of licensed beds and from a laboratory must list the number of generating employees.</p>
          <p>(b) The management plan must describe, to the extent the information is applicable to the facility:</p>
          <p>(1) the type of infectious waste and pathological waste that the person generates or handles;</p>
          <p>(2) the segregation, packaging, labeling, collection, storage, and transportation procedures for the infectious waste or pathological waste that will be followed;</p>
          <p>(3) the decontamination or disposal methods for the infectious or pathological waste that will be used;</p>
          <p>(4) the transporters and disposal facilities that will be used for the infectious waste;</p>
          <p>(5) the steps that will be taken to minimize the exposure of employees to infectious agents throughout the process of disposing of infectious or pathological wastes; and</p>
          <p>(6) the name of the individual responsible for the management of the infectious waste or pathological waste.</p>
          <p>(c) If the generator mails sharps for storage, decontamination, or disposal, the plan must specify how the generator will comply with applicable federal laws and rules. The plan must also specify the name, address, and telephone number of the facility to which the sharps are mailed, the name of the person who receives the sharps at the facility, and the annual amount mailed to the facility. If the facility to which the sharps are mailed is not the disposal facility, the plan must also identify the disposal facility.</p>
          <p>(d) The management plan must be kept at the facility.</p>
          <p>(e) To the extent applicable to the facility, management plans must be accompanied by a statement of the quantity of infectious and pathological waste generated, decontaminated, stored, incinerated, or disposed of at the facility during the previous two-year period. Quantities shall be reported in pounds.</p>
          <p>(f) A management plan must be updated at least once every two years.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.79.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Complying with management plans.</span></h2>
          <p>A person who prepares a management plan must comply with the management plan.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.79.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/1/1/#laws.9.75.0">1Sp1993 c 1 art 9 s 75</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.79.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Plans for storage, decontamination, incineration, and disposal facilities.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A person who stores, incinerates, or decontaminates infectious or pathological waste, other than at the facility where the waste was generated, or a person who incinerates infectious or pathological waste on site, must submit a copy of the management plan to the commissioner of the Pollution Control Agency with a fee of $225. The fee must be deposited in the state treasury and credited to the general fund.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner shall review the plans and may require a plan to be modified within 180 days after the plan is submitted if the commissioner determines that the plan is not consistent with state or federal law or that the plan is not adequate to minimize exposure of persons to the waste.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.79.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/337/#laws.0.5.0">1989 c 337 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/344/#laws.0.4.0">1991 c 344 s 4</a>-6; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/1/1/#laws.9.5.0">1Sp1993 c 1 art 9 s 5</a>,6; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/585/#laws.0.37.0">1994 c 585 s 37</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116.80">
        <h1 class="shn">116.80 TRANSPORTING INFECTIOUS WASTE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.80.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Transferring infectious waste.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A generator may not transfer infectious waste to a commercial transporter unless the transporter is registered with the commissioner.</p>
          <p>(b) A transporter may not deliver infectious waste to a facility prohibited to accept the waste.</p>
          <p>(c) A person who is registered to transport infectious waste may not refuse waste generated from a facility that is properly packaged and labeled.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.80.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Preparing management plans.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A commercial transporter in charge of a business that transports infectious waste must prepare a management plan for the infectious waste handled by the commercial transporter.</p>
          <p>(b) The management plan must describe, to the extent the information is applicable to the commercial transporter:</p>
          <p>(1) the type of infectious waste that the commercial transporter handles;</p>
          <p>(2) the transportation procedures for the infectious waste that will be followed;</p>
          <p>(3) the disposal facilities that will be used for the infectious waste;</p>
          <p>(4) the steps that will be taken to minimize the exposure of employees to infectious agents throughout the process of transporting and disposing of infectious waste; and</p>
          <p>(5) the name of the individual responsible for the transportation and management of the infectious waste.</p>
          <p>(c) The management plan must be kept at the commercial transporter's principal place of business.</p>
          <p>(d) Management plans must be accompanied by a statement of the quantity of infectious waste transported during the previous two-year period. Quantities shall be reported in pounds.</p>
          <p>(e) A management plan must be updated and resubmitted at least once every two years.</p>
          <p>(f) The commissioner shall review the plans and may require a plan to be modified within 180 days after the plan is submitted if the commissioner determines that the plan is not consistent with state or federal law or that the plan is not adequate to minimize exposure of persons to the waste.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.80.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Registration required.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A commercial transporter must register with the commissioner.</p>
          <p>(b) To register, a commercial transporter must submit a copy of the management plan to the commissioner of the Pollution Control Agency with a fee of $225. The fee must be deposited in the state treasury and credited to the general fund.</p>
          <p>(c) The registration is valid for two years.</p>
          <p>(d) The commissioner shall issue a registration card with a unique registration number to a person who has submitted a transporter's management plan unless the commissioner finds that registrant has outstanding unresolved violations of this section or a history of serious violations of chapter 115, 115A, 115B, or 116. The registration card must include the date the card expires.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.80.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Waste from other states.</span></h2>
          <p>A person may not transport infectious waste into the state for decontamination, storage, incineration, or disposal without complying with sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.76">116.76</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.82">116.82</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.80.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/337/#laws.0.6.0">1989 c 337 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/344/#laws.0.7.0">1991 c 344 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/1/1/#laws.9.7.0">1Sp1993 c 1 art 9 s 7</a>,8</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116.801">
        <h1 class="shn">116.801 INCINERATING INFECTIOUS WASTE; PERMIT REQUIRED.</h1>
        <p>(a) Except as provided in paragraph (b), a person may not construct, or expand the capacity of, a facility for the incineration of infectious waste, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.76">116.76</a>, without having obtained an air emission permit from the agency.</p>
        <p>(b) This section does not affect permit requirements under the rules of the agency for an incinerator that is upgraded to meet pollution control standards or an incinerator with a capacity of 350 pounds or less per hour that is planned to manage waste generated primarily by the owner or operator of the incinerator.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.801.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/231/#laws.0.1.0">1991 c 231 s 1</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116.802">
        <h1 class="shn">116.802 INCINERATING INFECTIOUS WASTE; ENVIRONMENTAL IMPACT.</h1>
        <p>Until the Pollution Control Agency adopts revisions to its air emission rules for incinerators, a new or expanded facility for the incineration of infectious waste that is subject to the permit requirement in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.801">116.801</a> may not receive a permit until an environmental impact statement for the facility has been prepared and approved. The Pollution Control Agency is the governmental unit responsible for preparation of an environmental impact statement required under this section.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.802.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/231/#laws.0.2.0">1991 c 231 s 2</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116.81">
        <h1 class="shn">116.81 RULES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.81.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Agency rules.</span></h2>
          <p>The agency, in consultation with the commissioner of health, may adopt rules to implement sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.76">116.76</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.82">116.82</a>. The agency, before adopting rules affecting animals or research animal waste, must consult the commissioner of agriculture and the Board of Animal Health.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.81.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/1/1/#laws.9.75.0">1Sp1993 c 1 art 9 s 75</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.81.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/337/#laws.0.7.0">1989 c 337 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/1/1/#laws.9.9.0">1Sp1993 c 1 art 9 s 9</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116.82">
        <h1 class="shn">116.82 AUTHORITY OF LOCAL GOVERNMENT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.82.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Preemption.</span></h2>
          <p>A county, municipality, or other political subdivision of the state may not adopt a definition of infectious or pathological waste that differs from the definitions in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.76">116.76</a>, or management requirements for infectious or pathological waste that differ from the requirements of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.78">116.78</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.79">116.79</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.82.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Local solid waste authority.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.76">116.76</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.81">116.81</a> do not affect local implementation of collection, storage, or disposal of solid waste that does not contain infectious waste.</p>
          <p>(b) Sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.76">116.76</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.81">116.81</a> do not affect county authority under other law to regulate and manage solid waste that does not contain infectious waste.</p>
          <p>(c) A political subdivision, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.03#stat.115A.03.24">115A.03, subdivision 24</a>, may not require a refuse-derived fuel facility to accept infectious waste.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.82.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Local enforcement.</span></h2>
          <p>Sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.76">116.76</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.81">116.81</a> may be enforced by a county by delegation of enforcement authority granted to the agency in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.83">116.83</a>. Separate enforcement actions may not be brought by a state agency and a county for the same violations. The state or county may not bring an action that is being enforced by the federal Office of Safety and Health Administration.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.82.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/337/#laws.0.8.0">1989 c 337 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/206/#laws.0.5.0">1993 c 206 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/1/1/#laws.9.10.0">1Sp1993 c 1 art 9 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/1/6/#laws.0.5.0">1Sp1993 c 6 s 5</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116.83">
        <h1 class="shn">116.83 ENFORCEMENT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.83.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Enforcement authority.</span></h2>
          <p>The agency may enforce sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.76">116.76</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.81">116.81</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.83.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/1/1/#laws.9.75.0">1Sp1993 c 1 art 9 s 75</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.83.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Access to information and property.</span></h2>
          <p>Subject to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/144.651">144.651</a>, the commissioner of the Pollution Control Agency may on presentation of credentials, during regular business hours:</p>
          <p>(1) examine and copy any books, records, memoranda, or data that is related to compliance with sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.76">116.76</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.81">116.81</a>; and</p>
          <p>(2) enter public or private property regulated by sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.76">116.76</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.81">116.81</a> for the purpose of taking an action authorized by this section including obtaining information and conducting investigations.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.83.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/337/#laws.0.9.0">1989 c 337 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/347/#laws.1.18.0">1991 c 347 art 1 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/1/1/#laws.9.11.0">1Sp1993 c 1 art 9 s 11</a>,12</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116.835">
        <h1 class="shn">116.835 SAFE SHARPS MANAGEMENT.</h1>
        <p>(a) A sharps manufacturer that sells or distributes sharps that are usually intended for home use and a pharmaceutical manufacturer that sells or distributes a medication in Minnesota that is usually intended to be self-injected in a home resulting in the generation of sharps shall, on or before July 1, 2011, post to its website a plan that describes how the manufacturer supports the safe collection and proper disposal of the sharps.</p>
        <p>(b) The plan required under paragraph (a) shall include, at a minimum, a description of the actions, if any, taken by the manufacturer to do the following:</p>
        <p>(1) provide for the safe collection and proper disposal of sharps;</p>
        <p>(2) educate consumers about safe management and collection opportunities; and</p>
        <p>(3) support efforts by retailers, pharmaceutical distributors, local governments, health care organizations, public health officers, solid waste service providers, organizations representing patients who use sharps, and other groups with interest in protecting public health and safety through the sale, collection, and proper disposal of sharps.</p>
        <p>(c) A public health agency or clinic that participates in a needle exchange program must post to its website a plan that describes how the agency or clinic supports the safe collection and proper disposal of the sharps.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.835.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/286/#laws.0.2.0">2010 c 286 s 2</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">MONITORS FOR INCINERATORS</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116.84">
        <h1 class="shn">116.84 MONITORS REQUIRED FOR PCB INCINERATORS.</h1>
        <p>Notwithstanding any other law to the contrary, an incinerator permit issued to a facility that allows burning of PCBs must, as a condition of the permit, require the installation of a continuous emission monitoring system approved by the commissioner. The monitoring system must provide continuous emission measurements to ensure optimum combustion efficiency of dioxin precursors. The system must also be capable of providing a permanent record of monitored emissions that will be available upon request to the commissioner and the general public. The commissioner shall provide periodic inspection of the monitoring system to determine its continued accuracy. Should, at any time, the permitted facility's emissions exceed permit requirements based on accurate and valid emissions data, the facility shall immediately commence shutdown of the incinerator until the appropriate modifications to the facility have been made to ensure its ability to meet permitted requirements.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.84.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/335/#laws.1.132.0">1989 c 335 art 1 s 132</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116.85">
        <h1 class="shn">116.85 MONITORS REQUIRED FOR OTHER INCINERATORS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.85.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Emission monitors.</span></h2>
          <p>Notwithstanding any other law to the contrary, an incinerator permit that contains emission limits for dioxin, cadmium, chromium, lead, or mercury must, as a condition of the permit, require the installation of an air emission monitoring system approved by the commissioner. The monitoring system must provide continuous measurements to ensure optimum combustion efficiency for the purpose of ensuring optimum dioxin destruction. The system shall also be capable of providing a permanent record of monitored emissions that will be available upon request to the commissioner and the general public. The commissioner shall provide periodic inspection of the monitoring system to determine its continued accuracy.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.85.1a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1a.<span class="headnote">Mercury testing.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Notwithstanding any other law to the contrary, a facility holding an incinerator permit that contains emission limits for mercury must, as a condition of the permit, conduct periodic stack testing for mercury as described by this subdivision. Hospital waste incinerators having a design capacity of less than 3,000,000 Btu per hour may use mercury segregation practices as an alternative to stack testing if allowed by applicable federal requirements, with the approval of the commissioner.</p>
          <p>(b) A facility shall conduct stack testing for mercury at intervals not to exceed three months. An incinerator facility burning greater than 30 percent by weight of refuse-derived fuel must conduct periodic stack testing for mercury at intervals not to exceed 12 months unless a previous test showed a permit exceedence after which the agency may require quarterly testing until permit requirements are satisfied. With the approval of the commissioner, an incinerator facility may use methods other than stack testing for determining mercury in air emissions.</p>
          <p>(c) After demonstrating that mercury emissions have been below 50 percent of the facility's permitted mercury limit for three consecutive years, as tested under the conditions of paragraph (b), an incinerator facility may choose to conduct stack testing once every three years or according to applicable federal requirements, whichever is more stringent. The facility shall notify the commissioner of its alternative mercury testing schedule, and the commissioner shall include operating conditions in the facility's permit that ensure that the facility will continue to emit mercury emissions less than 50 percent of the applicable standard.</p>
          <p>(d) The provisions of paragraph (c) allowing for less frequent stack testing for mercury apply to a new unit constructed at a facility that has previously met the requirements of paragraph (c), provided that the new unit demonstrates, as tested under the conditions of paragraph (b), that mercury emissions have been below 50 percent of the new unit's permitted mercury limit for one year.</p>
          <p>(e) If a test conducted under the provisions of paragraph (c) shows mercury emissions greater than 50 percent of the facility's permitted mercury limit, the facility shall conduct annual mercury stack sampling until emissions are below 50 percent of the facility's permitted mercury limit. Once the facility demonstrates that mercury emissions are again below 50 percent of the facility's permitted mercury limit, the facility may resume testing every three years or according to federal requirements, whichever is more stringent, upon notifying the commissioner.</p>
          <p>(f) In amending, modifying, or reissuing a facility's air emissions permit which contains a provision that restricts mercury emissions from the facility the commissioner shall, at a minimum, continue that permit restriction at the same level unless the applicant demonstrates that no good cause exists to do so.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.85.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Continuously monitored emissions.</span></h2>
          <p>Should, at any time after normal startup, the permitted facility's continuously monitored emissions exceed permit requirements, based on accurate and valid emissions data, the facility shall immediately report the exceedance to the commissioner and immediately either commence appropriate modifications to the facility to ensure its ability to meet permitted requirements or commence shutdown if the modifications cannot be completed within 72 hours. Compliance with permit requirements must then be demonstrated based on additional testing.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.85.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Periodically tested emissions.</span></h2>
          <p>Should, at any time after normal startup, the permitted facility's periodically tested emissions exceed permit requirements based on accurate and valid emissions data, the facility shall immediately report the exceedance to the commissioner, shall undertake appropriate steps to ensure the facility's compliance with permitted requirements, and shall demonstrate compliance within 60 days of the initial report of the exceedance. If the commissioner determines that compliance has not been achieved within 60 days, then the facility shall shut down until compliance with permit requirements is demonstrated based on additional testing.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.85.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Other law.</span></h2>
          <p>This section shall not be construed to limit the authority of the agency to regulate incinerator operations under any other law.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.85.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/335/#laws.1.133.0">1989 c 335 art 1 s 133</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/594/#laws.1.54.0">1990 c 594 art 1 s 54</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/189/#laws.0.1.0">1997 c 189 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/235/#laws.0.2.0">1999 c 235 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/213/#laws.0.1.0">2010 c 213 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116.86"><b>116.86</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/254/#laws.2.48.0">1991 c 254 art 2 s 48</a>]</div>
      <h1 class="fgh">RESIDENTIAL LEAD PAINT WASTE</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116.87">
        <h1 class="shn">116.87 DEFINITIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.87.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Residential lead paint waste.</span></h2>
          <p>"Residential lead paint waste" means waste produced by removing lead paint from the interior or exterior structure or the ground surface of a residence. Residential lead paint waste does not include:</p>
          <p>(1) lead paint waste removed with the aid of any chemical paint stripper; or</p>
          <p>(2) lead paint waste that is mixed with water and that contains any free liquid.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.87.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Residence.</span></h2>
          <p>The term "residence" has the meaning given in rules adopted under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/144.9501">144.9501</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/144.9512">144.9512</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.87.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/1/1/#laws.9.13.0">1Sp1993 c 1 art 9 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/213/#laws.1.2.0">1995 c 213 art 1 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/147/#laws.16.20.0">2007 c 147 art 16 s 20</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116.875">
        <h1 class="shn">116.875 AUTHORIZED MANAGEMENT METHODS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.875.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Disposal.</span></h2>
          <p>Notwithstanding any other law, a person who disposes of residential lead paint waste in the state may dispose of the waste at:</p>
          <p>(1) a land disposal facility that meets the requirements of Minnesota Rules, chapter 7045;</p>
          <p>(2) a facility that meets the requirements for a new mixed municipal solid waste land disposal facility under Minnesota Rules, chapter 7035, that began operation after January 1, 1989;</p>
          <p>(3) a demolition debris land disposal facility equipped with a clay or artificial liner and leachate collection system; or</p>
          <p>(4) a solid waste incinerator ash landfill if disposal is approved by the commissioner in accordance with agency rules.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.875.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Management responsibility; not transferable to occupant.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A person whose activities produce residential lead paint waste is responsible for the management and proper disposal of the waste.</p>
          <p>(b) When residential lead paint waste is produced by activities of a person other than the occupant of the residence from which the waste is removed, the person shall not leave the residential lead paint waste at that residence and shall not transfer responsibility for managing or disposing of the waste to the occupant.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.875.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Waste produced by occupant.</span></h2>
          <p>Residential lead paint waste produced by activities of the occupant of the residence from which the waste is removed must be managed as provided by law for household hazardous waste.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.875.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Demolition debris.</span></h2>
          <p>Residential lead paint waste attached to woodwork, walls, or other elements removed from the structure of a residence that constitute demolition debris may be disposed of at any permitted demolition debris land disposal facility.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.875.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/1/1/#laws.9.14.0">1Sp1993 c 1 art 9 s 14</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116.88">
        <h1 class="shn">116.88 PROHIBITED METHODS OF MANAGEMENT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.88.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Unlined landfills.</span></h2>
          <p>Except as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.875#stat.116.875.4">116.875, subdivision 4</a>, no person shall dispose of residential lead paint waste at an unlined land disposal facility.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.88.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Incineration.</span></h2>
          <p>No person shall send or accept residential lead paint waste for incineration by a mixed municipal solid waste incinerator.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.88.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/1/1/#laws.9.15.0">1Sp1993 c 1 art 9 s 15</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116.885">
        <h1 class="shn">116.885 RECYCLING AND TREATMENT.</h1>
        <p>Nothing in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.87">116.87</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.89">116.89</a> is intended to prevent or discourage treatment or recycling of residential lead paint waste. The commissioner shall encourage treatment and recycling of residential lead paint waste.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.885.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/1/1/#laws.9.16.0">1Sp1993 c 1 art 9 s 16</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116.89">
        <h1 class="shn">116.89 ENFORCEMENT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.89.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Rules.</span></h2>
          <p>The Minnesota Pollution Control Agency may adopt rules necessary to implement and enforce the provisions of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.87">116.87</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.885">116.885</a>, including rules to regulate the transportation, storage, disposal, and other management of residential lead paint waste after the waste leaves the site where it was produced.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.89.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">License revocation.</span></h2>
          <p>In addition to enforcement by the Minnesota Pollution Control Agency, the commissioner of health may revoke the license of an abatement contractor that violates any provision of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.87">116.87</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.885">116.885</a> or the rules adopted under subdivision 1.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.89.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/1/1/#laws.9.17.0">1Sp1993 c 1 art 9 s 17</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">CITIZEN REPORTS</h1>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116.90"><b>116.90</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/248/#laws.0.19.0">2014 c 248 s 19</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116.91">
        <h1 class="shn">116.91 CITIZEN REPORTS OF ENVIRONMENTAL VIOLATIONS.</h1>
        <p>The agency shall maintain and publicize a toll-free number to enable citizens to report information about potential environmental violations. The agency may establish a program to pay awards from funds raised from private sources to persons who provide information that leads to the conviction for an environmental crime.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.91.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/347/#laws.3.2.0">1991 c 347 art 3 s 2</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">MERCURY CONTAMINATION; REDUCTION</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116.915">
        <h1 class="shn">116.915 MERCURY REDUCTION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.915.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Goal.</span></h2>
          <p>It is the goal of the state to reduce mercury contamination by reducing the release of mercury into the air and water of the state by 60 percent from 1990 levels by December 31, 2000, and by 70 percent from 1990 levels by December 31, 2005. The goal applies to the statewide total of releases from existing and new sources of mercury. The commissioner shall publish updated estimates of 1990 releases in the State Register.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.915.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Reduction strategies.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall implement the strategies recommended by the Mercury Contamination Reduction Initiative Advisory Council and identified on pages 31 to 42 of the Minnesota Pollution Control Agency's report entitled "Report on the Mercury Contamination Reduction Initiative Advisory Council's Results and Recommendations" as transmitted to the legislature by the commissioner's letter dated March 15, 1999. The commissioner shall solicit, by July 1, 1999, voluntary reduction agreements from sources that emit more than 50 pounds of mercury per year.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.915.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.</h2>
          <p>[Obsolete, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.2.161.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 2 s 161</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.915.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/231/#laws.0.150.0">1999 c 231 s 150</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116.92">
        <h1 class="shn">116.92 MERCURY EMISSIONS; REDUCTION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.92.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Sales.</span></h2>
          <p>A person may not sell mercury to another person in this state without providing a material safety data sheet, as defined in United States Code, title 42, section 11049, and requiring the purchaser to sign a statement that the purchaser:</p>
          <p>(1) will use the mercury only for a medical, dental, instructional, research, or manufacturing purpose; and</p>
          <p>(2) understands the toxicity of mercury and will appropriately store and use it and will not place, or allow anyone under the purchaser's control to place, the mercury in the solid waste stream or in a wastewater disposal system, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115.01#stat.115.01.4">115.01, subdivision 4</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.92.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Using mercury.</span></h2>
          <p>A person who uses mercury in any application may not place, or deliver the mercury to another person who places residues, particles, scrapings, or other materials that contain mercury in solid waste or wastewater, except for traces of materials that may inadvertently pass through a filtration system during a dental procedure.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.92.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Labeling; products containing mercury.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A manufacturer or wholesaler may not sell and a retailer may not knowingly sell any of the following items in this state that contain mercury unless the item is labeled in a manner to clearly inform a purchaser or consumer that mercury is present in the item and that the item may not be placed in the garbage until the mercury is removed and reused, recycled, or otherwise managed to ensure that it does not become part of solid waste or wastewater:</p>
          <p>(1) a thermostat or thermometer;</p>
          <p>(2) an electric switch, individually or as part of another product, other than a motor vehicle;</p>
          <p>(3) an appliance;</p>
          <p>(4) a medical or scientific instrument;</p>
          <p>(5) an electric relay or other electrical device;</p>
          <p>(6) a fluorescent or high-intensity discharge lamp, individually or as part of another product; and</p>
          <p>(7) laboratory chemicals, reagents, fixatives, and electrodes.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) Labeling of items in accordance with mercury product labeling plans approved by another state that is a member of the Interstate Mercury Education and Reduction Clearinghouse (IMERC) shall be considered to be in compliance with this section. The manufacturer shall provide a copy of the labeling plan to the agency and shall notify the agency if the approval is modified.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) Manufacturers of products that contain a mercury-containing lamp not intended to be replaceable by the user or consumer shall meet the product labeling requirements of this section by placing the label on the product or in the care and use manual or product instructions.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.92.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Removing from service; products containing mercury.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) When an item listed in this section is removed from service, the mercury in the item must be reused, recycled, or otherwise managed to ensure compliance with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.932">115A.932</a>.</p>
          <p>(b) A person who is in the business of replacing or repairing an item listed in this section in households shall ensure, or deliver the item to a facility that will ensure, that the mercury contained in an item that is replaced or repaired is reused or recycled or otherwise managed in compliance with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.932">115A.932</a>.</p>
          <p>(c) A person may not crush a motor vehicle unless the person has first made a good faith effort to remove all of the mercury switches in the motor vehicle.</p>
          <p>(d) An item managed according to the requirements of this section must be transported in a container designed to prevent the escape of mercury into the environment by volatilization or any other means.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.92.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Thermostats.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The definitions in this paragraph apply to this subdivision:</p>
          <p>(1) "contractor" means a person engaged in the business of installing, servicing, or removing thermostats and other heating, ventilation, and air conditioning components, including a contractor removing thermostats in conjunction with renovation and demolition activities in accordance with Minnesota Rules, part <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/7035.0805">7035.0805</a>;</p>
          <p>(2) "qualified contractor" means a contractor:</p>
          <p>(i) who employs seven or more service technicians or installers;</p>
          <p>(ii) who is located in an area outside of an urban area, as defined by the United States Census Bureau; or</p>
          <p>(iii) whose primary business consists of renovation and demolition activities;</p>
          <p>(3) "retailer" means a person who sells thermostats of any kind directly to homeowners or other end-users through any selling or distribution mechanism;</p>
          <p>(4) "thermostat" means a temperature control device that may contain elemental mercury in a sealed component that serves as a switch or temperature-sensing element and a sealed component that has been removed from a temperature control device; and</p>
          <p>(5) "wholesaler" means a person engaged in the distribution and wholesale sale of thermostats and other heating, ventilation, and air conditioning components to contractors who install heating, ventilation, and air conditioning components.</p>
          <p>(b) A manufacturer of thermostats that contain mercury or that may replace thermostats that contain mercury is responsible for the costs of collecting and managing the replaced mercury-containing thermostats to ensure that the thermostats do not become part of the solid waste stream.</p>
          <p>(c) A manufacturer of thermostats that contain mercury or that may replace thermostats that contain mercury shall, in addition to the requirements of subdivision 3, provide incentives for and sufficient information to purchasers and consumers of the thermostats for the purchasers or consumers to ensure that mercury in thermostats being removed from service is reused or recycled or otherwise managed in compliance with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.932">115A.932</a>. A manufacturer that has complied with this subdivision is not liable for improper disposal by purchasers or consumers of thermostats.</p>
          <p>(d) A manufacturer of thermostats subject to this subdivision, or an organization of manufacturers of thermostats and its officers, members, employees, and agents, may participate in projects or programs to collect and properly manage waste thermostats. Any person who participates in a project or program is immune from liability under state law relating to antitrust, restraint of trade, unfair trade practices, and other regulation of trade or commerce for activities related to the collection and management of the thermostats under this subdivision.</p>
          <p>(e) A manufacturer of thermostats or organization of manufacturers of thermostats that participates in a thermostat collection and management program under this subdivision must report at least annually to the agency. The report must include:</p>
          <p>(1) a description of how the program operates;</p>
          <p>(2) a description of program components, including incentives provided under this subdivision, and an evaluation of the program components' effectiveness in promoting participation and recovery of thermostats;</p>
          <p>(3) eligibility criteria for program participants;</p>
          <p>(4) a list of program participants; and</p>
          <p>(5) the number of thermostats remitted by each program participant during the reporting period.</p>
          <p>(f) A wholesaler, qualified contractor, or retailer may participate as a collection site in a manufacturer's mercury thermostat collection and management program required under this subdivision. A wholesaler or retailer that participates as a collection site in a manufacturer's mercury thermostat collection and management program shall post prominent signs at the wholesaler's or retailer's business location regarding the collection and management of mercury thermostats.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.92.5a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5a.<span class="headnote">Displacement relays.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A manufacturer of a displacement relay that contains mercury is responsible for the costs of collecting and managing its displacement relays to ensure that the relays do not become part of the solid waste stream.</p>
          <p>(b) A manufacturer of a displacement relay that contains mercury shall, in addition to the requirements of subdivision 3, provide incentives for, and sufficient information to, purchasers and consumers of the relay to ensure that the relay does not become part of the waste stream. A manufacturer that has complied with this subdivision is not liable for improper disposal by purchasers or consumers of its relays.</p>
          <p>(c) A manufacturer subject to this subdivision, or an organization of such manufacturers and its officers, members, employees, and agents, may participate in projects or programs to collect and properly manage waste displacement relays. Any person who participates in such a project or program is immune from liability under state law relating to antitrust, restraint of trade, unfair trade practices, and other regulation of trade or commerce for activities related to the collection and management of the relays under this subdivision.</p>
          <p>(d) For the purposes of this subdivision, a "displacement relay" means an electric flow control device having one or more poles that contain metallic mercury and a plunger which, when energized by a magnetic field, moves into a pool of mercury, displacing the mercury sufficiently to create a closed electrical circuit.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.92.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Mercury thermometers prohibited.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A manufacturer, wholesaler, or retailer may not sell or distribute at no cost a thermometer containing mercury that was manufactured after June 1, 2001.</p>
          <p>(b) Paragraph (a) does not apply to an electronic thermometer with a battery containing mercury if the battery is in compliance with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/325E.125">325E.125</a>.</p>
          <p>(c) A manufacturer is in compliance with this subdivision if the manufacturer:</p>
          <p>(1) has received an exclusion or exemption from a state that is a member of the Interstate Mercury Education and Reduction Clearinghouse (IMERC) for replacement parts when no alternative is available or for an application when no feasible alternative is available;</p>
          <p>(2) submits a copy of the approved exclusion or exemption to the commissioner; and</p>
          <p>(3) meets all of the requirements in the approved exclusion or exemption for the manufacturer's activities within the state.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.92.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Fluorescent and high-intensity discharge lamps; large use applications.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A person who sells fluorescent or high-intensity discharge lamps that contain mercury to the owner or manager of an industrial, commercial, office, or multiunit residential building, or to any person who replaces or removes from service outdoor lamps that contain mercury, shall clearly inform the purchaser in writing on the invoice for the lamps, or in a separate writing, that the lamps contain mercury, a hazardous substance that is regulated by federal or state law and that they may not be placed in solid waste. This paragraph does not apply to a person who incidentally sells fluorescent or high-intensity discharge lamps at retail to the specified purchasers.</p>
          <p>(b) A person who contracts with the owner or manager of an industrial, commercial, office, or multiunit residential building, or with a person responsible for outdoor lighting, to remove from service fluorescent or high-intensity discharge lamps that contain mercury shall clearly inform, in writing, the person for whom the work is being done that the lamps being removed from service contain mercury and what the contractor's arrangements are for the management of the mercury in the removed lamps.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.92.7a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7a.<span class="headnote">Fluorescent and high-intensity discharge lamps; residential applications.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Any information regarding fluorescent and high-intensity discharge lamps containing mercury that is sent by a utility to a customer, present on a utility's website, or contained in a utility's print, radio, or video advertisement, must:</p>
          <p>(1) state that the lamps contain mercury;</p>
          <p>(2) state that mercury is harmful to the environment;</p>
          <p>(3) state that placing the lamps in garbage is illegal; and</p>
          <p>(4) provide a toll-free telephone number or website that customers can access to learn how to lawfully dispose of the lamps.</p>
          <p>(b) The information under paragraph (a) must be:</p>
          <p>(1) provided in a minimum of 12-point type in print or online media; and</p>
          <p>(2) provided in a manner that the ordinary consumer will understand that fluorescent and high-intensity discharge lamps contain mercury and must not be placed in garbage in Minnesota.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) A television or radio advertisement regarding fluorescent and high-intensity discharge lamps containing mercury must prominently convey the information that the lamps contain mercury and must be recycled.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.92.7b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7b.<span class="headnote">Ban; mercury-containing general purpose lighting.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) For purposes of this subdivision, the following terms have the meanings given:</p>
          <p>(1) "compact fluorescent lamp" means a compact low-pressure, mercury-containing, electric-discharge light source:</p>
          <p>(i) of any tube diameter or tube length;</p>
          <p>(ii) of any lamp size or shape for directional and nondirectional installations, including but not limited to PL, spiral, twin tube, triple twin, 2D, U-bend, and circular;</p>
          <p>(iii) in which a fluorescent coating transforms some of the ultraviolet energy generated by the mercury discharge into visible light;</p>
          <p>(iv) that has one base or end cap of any type, including but not limited to screw, bayonet, two pins, and four pins;</p>
          <p>(v) that is integrally ballasted or non-integrally ballasted; and</p>
          <p>(vi) that has light emission between a correlated color temperature of 1700K and 24000K and a Duv of +0.024 and -0.024 in the International Commission on Illumination (CIE) Uniform Color Space (CAM02-UCS);</p>
          <p>(2) "linear fluorescent lamp" means a low-pressure, mercury-containing, electric-discharge light source:</p>
          <p>(i) of any tube diameter, including but not limited to T5, T8, T10, and T12;</p>
          <p>(ii) with a tube length from 0.5 to 8.0 feet, inclusive;</p>
          <p>(iii) of any lamp shape, including but not limited to linear, U-bend, and circular;</p>
          <p>(iv) in which a fluorescent coating transforms some of the ultraviolet energy generated by the mercury discharge into visible light;</p>
          <p>(v) that has two bases or end caps of any type, including but not limited to single-pin, two-pin, and recessed double contact; and</p>
          <p>(vi) that has light emission between a correlated color temperature of 1700K and 24000K and a Duv of +0.024 and -0.024 in the CIE CAM02-UCS;</p>
          <p>(3) "mercury vapor lamp" means a high-intensity discharge lamp, including clear, phosphor-coated, and self-ballasted screw base lamps, in which the major portion of the light is produced by radiation from mercury typically operating at a partial vapor pressure in excess of 100,000 pascals;</p>
          <p>(4) "mercury vapor lamp ballast" means a device that is designed and marketed to start and operate mercury vapor lamps intended for general illumination by providing the necessary voltage and current; and</p>
          <p>(5) "specialty application mercury vapor lamp ballast" means a mercury vapor lamp ballast:</p>
          <p>(i) that is designed and marketed for operating mercury vapor lamps used in quality inspection, industrial processing, or scientific applications, including fluorescent microscopy and ultraviolet curing; and</p>
          <p>(ii) the label of which states "For specialty applications only, not for general illumination" and indicates the specific applications for which the ballast is designed.</p>
