          <p>(b) Effective January 1, 2025, a person may not sell, offer for sale, or distribute in the state as a new manufactured product a screw- or bayonet-base type compact fluorescent lamp, a mercury vapor lamp, or a mercury vapor lamp ballast, whether sold separately, in a retrofit kit, or in a luminaire. Effective January 1, 2026, a person may not sell, offer for sale, or distribute in the state as a new manufactured product a pin-base type compact fluorescent lamp or a linear fluorescent lamp.</p>
          <p>(c) This subdivision does not apply to:</p>
          <p>(1) a lamp designed and marketed exclusively for image capture and projection, including for:</p>
          <p>(i) photocopying;</p>
          <p>(ii) printing, directly or in preprocessing;</p>
          <p>(iii) lithography;</p>
          <p>(iv) film and video projection; or</p>
          <p>(v) holography;</p>
          <p>(2) a lamp that has a high proportion of ultraviolet light emission and that:</p>
          <p>(i) has high ultraviolet content and ultraviolet power greater than two milliwatts per kilolumen;</p>
          <p>(ii) is for germicidal use, such as for destroying DNA, and emits a peak radiation of approximately 253.7 nanometers;</p>
          <p>(iii) is designed and marketed exclusively for disinfection or fly-trapping and from which:</p>
          <p>(A) the radiation power emitted between 250 and 315 nanometers represents at least five percent of the total radiation power emitted between 250 and 800 nanometers; or</p>
          <p>(B) the radiation power emitted between 315 and 400 nanometers represents at least 20 percent of the total radiation power emitted between 250 and 800 nanometers;</p>
          <p>(iv) is designed and marketed exclusively for generating ozone when the primary purpose is to emit radiation at approximately 185.1 nanometers;</p>
          <p>(v) is designed and marketed exclusively for coral zooxanthellae symbiosis and from which the radiation power emitted between 400 and 480 nanometers represents at least 40 percent of the total radiation power emitted between 250 and 800 nanometers; or</p>
          <p>(vi) is designed and marketed exclusively for use in a sunlamp product, as defined in Code of Federal Regulations, title 21, section 1040.20(b)(9) (2022);</p>
          <p>(3) specialty application mercury vapor lamp ballasts; or</p>
          <p>(4) a compact fluorescent lamp used to replace a lamp in a motor vehicle if the motor vehicle was manufactured on or before January 1, 2020.</p>
          <p>(d) Nothing in this section limits the ability of a utility to offer energy-efficient lighting, rebates, or lamp-recycling services or to claim energy savings resulting from such programs through the utility's energy conservation and optimization plans approved by the commissioner of commerce under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216B.241">216B.241</a> or an energy conservation and optimization plan filed by a consumer-owned utility under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216B.2403">216B.2403</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.92.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Ban; toys, games, and apparel.</span></h2>
          <p>A person may not sell for resale or at retail in this state a toy or game that contains mercury, or an item of clothing or wearing apparel that is exempt from sales tax under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/297A.67#stat.297A.67.8">297A.67, subdivision 8</a>, that contains an electric switch that contains mercury.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.92.8a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8a.<span class="headnote">Ban; mercury manometers.</span></h2>
          <p>After June 30, 1997, mercury manometers for use on dairy farms may not be sold or installed, nor may mercury manometers in use on dairy farms be repaired. After December 31, 2000, all mercury manometers on dairy farms must be removed from use.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.92.8b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8b.<span class="headnote">Ban; mercury-containing sphygmomanometers.</span></h2>
          <p>After August 1, 2007, a person may not sell, offer for sale, distribute, install, or reinstall in the state a sphygmomanometer containing mercury.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.92.8c">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8c.<span class="headnote">Ban; mercury-containing gastrointestinal devices.</span></h2>
          <p>After August 1, 2007, a person may not sell, offer for sale, distribute, or use in the state an esophageal dilator, bougie tube, gastrointestinal tube, feeding tube, or similar device containing mercury.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.92.8d">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8d.<span class="headnote">Ban; mercury-containing thermostats.</span></h2>
          <p>After August 1, 2007, a person may not sell, offer for sale, distribute, install, or reinstall in the state a product or device that uses a mercury switch to sense and control room temperature through communication with heating, ventilating, or air-conditioning equipment. This subdivision does not apply to a thermostat used to sense and control temperature as part of a manufacturing process.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.92.8e">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8e.<span class="headnote">Ban; mercury-containing switches and relays.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) After August 1, 2007, a person may not sell, offer for sale, or distribute in the state a mercury switch or mercury relay individually or as part of another product.</p>
          <p>(b) For the purposes of this subdivision:</p>
          <p>(1) "mercury relay" means a mercury-containing product or device that opens or closes electrical contacts to affect the operation of other devices in the same or another electrical circuit and includes, but is not limited to, mercury displacement relays, mercury wetted reed relays, and mercury contact relays; and</p>
          <p>(2) "mercury switch" means a mercury-containing product or device that opens or closes an electrical circuit or gas valve and includes, but is not limited to, mercury float switches actuated by rising or falling liquid levels, mercury tilt switches actuated by a change in the switch position, mercury pressure switches actuated by a change in pressure, mercury temperature switches actuated by a change in temperature, and mercury flame sensors. A mercury switch does not include a mercury-added thermostat or a mercury diostat.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) A manufacturer shall be in compliance with this subdivision if:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) it has received an exclusion or exemption from a state that is a member of the Interstate Mercury Education and Reduction Clearinghouse (IMERC) for replacement parts or for a use where no feasible alternative is available;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) it submits a copy of the approved exclusion or exemption to the commissioner; and</p>
          <p class="in">(3) it meets all of the requirements in the approved exclusion or exemption for its activities within the state.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.92.8f">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8f.<span class="headnote">Ban; mercury diostats.</span></h2>
          <p>After January 1, 2008, a person may not sell, offer for sale, or distribute a new gas oven, range, or stove containing a mercury-containing switch that controls a gas valve in an oven or oven portion of a gas range or stove.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.92.8g">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8g.<span class="headnote">Ban; mercury-containing barometers, manometers, and pyrometers.</span></h2>
          <p>After January 1, 2008, a person may not sell, offer for sale, or distribute in the state a mercury-containing device used for measuring atmospheric pressure or for measuring pressure of liquids and gases or a mercury-containing device used for measuring the temperature of extremely hot materials, individually or as part of another product.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.92.8h">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8h.<span class="headnote">Ban; mercury in over-the-counter pharmaceuticals.</span></h2>
          <p>After January 1, 2008, a person may not sell, offer for sale, or distribute in the state for human use an over-the-counter pharmaceutical product containing mercury.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.92.8i">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8i.<span class="headnote">Ban; mercury in cosmetics, toiletries, and fragrances.</span></h2>
          <p>After January 1, 2008, a person may not sell, offer for sale, or distribute in the state a cosmetic, toiletry, or fragrance product containing mercury.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.92.8j">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8j.<span class="headnote">Exclusion for existing equipment.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">The prohibitions in subdivisions 6 and 8b to 8g do not apply if a thermometer, switch, relay, or measuring device is used to replace a thermometer, switch, relay, or measuring device that is a component of an industrial measurement system or control system until the system is replaced or a nonmercury component for the system is available. The owner of the system shall notify the commissioner within 30 days of replacing the component and identify the replacement mercury component that was installed.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.92.8k">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8k.<span class="headnote">Ban; mercury in balancing and dampening products and equipment.</span></h2>
          <p>A person may not sell, offer for sale, distribute, install, or use in the state a mercury-containing product or mercury-containing equipment that is used for balancing, dampening, or providing a weight or counterweight function.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.92.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Enforcement; generators of household hazardous waste.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A violation of subdivision 2 or 4, paragraph (a), by a generator of household hazardous waste, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.96">115A.96</a>, or a violation of subdivision 8 by a person selling at retail, is not subject to enforcement under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115.071#stat.115.071.3">115.071, subdivision 3</a>.</p>
          <p>(b) An administrative penalty imposed under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.072">116.072</a> for a violation of subdivision 2 or 4, paragraph (a), by a generator of household hazardous waste, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.96">115A.96</a>, or for a violation of subdivision 8 by a person selling at retail, may not exceed $700.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.92.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Definition of mercury-containing.</span></h2>
          <p>For the purposes of this section, "mercury-containing" or "containing mercury" means that the product, component of a product, or chemical formulation contains intentionally added mercury.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.92.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/560/#laws.0.3.0">1992 c 560 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/603/#laws.0.37.0">1992 c 603 s 37</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/249/#laws.0.28.0">1993 c 249 s 28</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/585/#laws.0.38.0">1994 c 585 s 38</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/247/#laws.1.43.0">1995 c 247 art 1 s 43</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/62/#laws.0.2.0">1997 c 62 s 2</a>,3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/216/#laws.0.116.0">1997 c 216 s 116</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/418/#laws.1.44.0">2000 c 418 art 1 s 44</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/47/#laws.0.1.0">2001 c 47 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2006/0/201/#laws.0.2.0">2006 c 201 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/109/#laws.0.2.0">2007 c 109 s 2</a>-13; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/277/#laws.0.2.0">2014 c 277 s 2</a>-6; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/116/#laws.2.22.0">2024 c 116 art 2 s 22</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116.921">
        <h1 class="shn">116.921 MULTISTATE CLEARINGHOUSE.</h1>
        <p class="in">The agency is authorized to participate in the Interstate Mercury Education and Reduction Clearinghouse (IMERC) to assist in carrying out the requirements and coordinating any other activities related to the administration of statutes governing the purchase, sale, use, labeling, disposal, and management of mercury and mercury-containing products.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.921.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/109/#laws.0.14.0">2007 c 109 s 14</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116.925">
        <h1 class="shn">116.925 ELECTRIC ENERGY; MERCURY EMISSIONS REPORT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.925.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Report.</span></h2>
          <p>To address the shared responsibility between the providers and consumers of electricity for the protection of Minnesota's lakes, each electric utility, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216B.38#stat.216B.38.5">216B.38, subdivision 5</a>, and each person that generates electricity in this state for that person's own use or for sale at retail or wholesale shall provide to the commissioner of the Pollution Control Agency by April 1 an annual report of the amount of mercury emitted in generating that electricity at that person's facilities for the previous calendar year.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.925.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Contents of report.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A report must include:</p>
          <p>(1) a list of all generation facilities owned or operated by the utility or person subject to subdivision 1;</p>
          <p>(2) all readily available information regarding the amount of electricity purchased by the utility or person subject to subdivision 1, for use in the state; and</p>
          <p>(3) information for each facility owned or operated by the utility or person subject to subdivision 1, stating: (i) the amount of electricity generated at the facility for use or for sale in this state at retail or wholesale; (ii) the amount of fuel used to generate that electricity at the facility; and (iii) the amount of mercury emitted in generating that electricity in the previous calendar year, based on emission factors, stack tests, fuel analysis, or other methods approved by the commissioner. The report must include the mercury content of the fuel if it is determined in conjunction with a stack test.</p>
          <p>(b) The following are de minimis standards for small and little-used generation facilities:</p>
          <p>(1) less than 240 hours of operation by the combustion unit per year;</p>
          <p>(2) a fuel capacity input at the combustion unit of less than 150,000,000 British thermal units per hour; or</p>
          <p>(3) an electrical generation unit with maximum output of less than or equal to 15 megawatts.</p>
          <p class="bl">A utility or person subject to this section who owns or operates a combustion unit that qualifies under one of these de minimis standards is not required to provide the information described in paragraph (a) for that combustion unit.</p>
          <p>(c) A report need not be filed for a combustion device for a year in which the device has documented mercury emissions of three pounds or less.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.925.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Report to consumers.</span></h2>
          <p>By January 1, 1999, and biennially thereafter in the report on air toxics required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115D.15">115D.15</a>, the commissioner shall report the amount of mercury emitted in the generation of electricity.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.925.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/191/#laws.2.2.0">1997 c 191 art 2 s 2</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116.93">
        <h1 class="shn">116.93 LAMP RECYCLING FACILITIES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.93.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definition.</span></h2>
          <p>For the purposes of this section, "lamp recycling facility" means a facility operated to remove, recover, and recycle for reuse mercury or other hazardous materials from fluorescent or high-intensity discharge lamps.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.93.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Lamp recycling facility; permits or licenses; reporting.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A person may not operate a lamp recycling facility without obtaining a permit or license for the facility from the agency. The permit or license must require:</p>
          <p>(1) a plan for response to releases, including emergency response;</p>
          <p>(2) proof of financial responsibility for closure and any necessary postclosure care at the facility which may include a performance bond or other insurance;</p>
          <p>(3) liability insurance or another financial mechanism that provides proof of financial responsibility for response actions required under chapter 115B; and</p>
          <p>(4) by March 1 each year, beginning in 2008, an annual report to the agency on the number and type of lamps received from businesses and households in the state and total number of lamps received from all generators outside of the state. The agency shall specify the format for the report under this clause and make the reported information available on the agency's website.</p>
          <p>(b) A lamp recycling facility that is licensed or permitted by a county under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/473.811#stat.473.811.5b">473.811, subdivision 5b</a>, complies with this subdivision if the license or permit held by the facility contains at least all the terms and conditions required by the agency for a license or permit issued under this subdivision.</p>
          <p>(c) A lamp recycling facility with a demonstrated capability for recycling that is in operation prior to adoption of rules for a licensing or permitting process for the facility by the agency may continue to operate in accordance with a compliance agreement or other approval by the commissioner until a license or permit is issued by the agency under this subdivision.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.93.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/249/#laws.0.29.0">1993 c 249 s 29</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/109/#laws.0.15.0">2007 c 109 s 15</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116.931">
        <h1 class="shn">116.931 WHEEL WEIGHTS AND BALANCING PRODUCTS; LEAD AND MERCURY PROHIBITION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.931.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) For the purposes of this section, the following terms have the meanings given.</p>
          <p>(b) "Motor vehicle" means a self-propelled vehicle or a vehicle propelled or drawn by a self-propelled vehicle that is operated on a highway, on a railroad track, on the ground, in the water, or in the air.</p>
          <p>(c) "New motor vehicle" means a motor vehicle that has not been previously sold to a person except a distributor, wholesaler, or motor vehicle dealer for resale.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.931.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Tire service.</span></h2>
          <p>When replacing or balancing a tire on a motor vehicle or aircraft, a person may not use a wheel weight or other product for balancing motor vehicle or aircraft wheels if the weight or other balancing product contains lead or mercury that was intentionally added during the manufacture of the product.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.931.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Sales ban.</span></h2>
          <p>A person may not sell or offer to sell or distribute weights or other products for balancing motor vehicle or aircraft wheels if the weight or other balancing product contains lead or mercury that was intentionally added during the manufacture of the product.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.931.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">New motor vehicles.</span></h2>
          <p>A person may not sell a new motor vehicle or aircraft that is equipped with a weight or other product for balancing wheels if the weight or other balancing product contains lead or mercury that was intentionally added during the manufacture of the product.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.931.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Salvage.</span></h2>
          <p>A person may not shred or crush, or market for shredding or crushing, any motor vehicle, aircraft, watercraft, or railroad or industrial equipment, or any portion thereof, without:</p>
          <p>(1) inspecting the vehicle or equipment; and</p>
          <p>(2) removing all weights or other products for balancing wheels or other equipment if the weights or balancing products contain lead or mercury that was intentionally added during the manufacture of the weights or balancing products.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.931.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Managing wheel weights and balancing products.</span></h2>
          <p>Mercury in wheel weights and other balancing products for motor vehicle and aircraft wheels must be recycled or otherwise managed to comply with sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.932">115A.932</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.92">116.92</a> and to ensure that it does not become part of the solid waste stream and is not released to the environment. Lead in wheel weights and other balancing products for motor vehicle and aircraft wheels must be recycled to ensure that it does not become part of the solid waste stream and is not released to the environment.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.931.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Educational materials; outreach.</span></h2>
          <p>Prior to January 1, 2016, the agency shall produce and distribute educational materials on the prohibitions required under this section to businesses subject to the prohibitions and shall conduct additional outreach and education activities to those businesses.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.931.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/277/#laws.0.7.0">2014 c 277 s 7</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116.94"><b>116.94</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/247/#laws.1.67.0">1995 c 247 art 1 s 67</a>]</div>
      <h1 class="fgh">CHEMICALS OF HIGH CONCERN</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116.9401">
        <h1 class="shn">116.9401 DEFINITIONS.</h1>
        <p>(a) For the purposes of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.9401">116.9401</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.9407">116.9407</a>, the following terms have the meanings given them.</p>
        <p>(b) "Agency" means the Pollution Control Agency.</p>
        <p>(c) "Alternative" means a substitute process, product, material, chemical, strategy, or combination of these that is technically feasible and serves a functionally equivalent purpose to a chemical in a children's product.</p>
        <p>(d) "Chemical" means a substance with a distinct molecular composition or a group of structurally related substances and includes the breakdown products of the substance or substances that form through decomposition, degradation, or metabolism.</p>
        <p>(e) "Chemical of high concern" means a chemical identified on the basis of credible scientific evidence by a state, federal, or international agency as being known or suspected with a high degree of probability to:</p>
        <p>(1) harm the normal development of a fetus or child or cause other developmental toxicity;</p>
        <p>(2) cause cancer, genetic damage, or reproductive harm;</p>
        <p>(3) disrupt the endocrine or hormone system;</p>
        <p>(4) damage the nervous system, immune system, or organs, or cause other systemic toxicity;</p>
        <p>(5) be persistent, bioaccumulative, and toxic; or</p>
        <p>(6) be very persistent and very bioaccumulative.</p>
        <p>(f) "Child" means a person under 12 years of age.</p>
        <p>(g) "Children's product" means a consumer product intended for use by children, such as baby products, toys, car seats, personal care products, and clothing.</p>
        <p>(h) "Commissioner" means the commissioner of the Pollution Control Agency.</p>
        <p>(i) "Department" means the Department of Health.</p>
        <p>(j) "Distributor" means a person who sells consumer products to retail establishments on a wholesale basis.</p>
        <p>(k) "Green chemistry" means an approach to designing and manufacturing products that minimizes the use and generation of toxic substances.</p>
        <p>(l) "Manufacturer" means any person who manufactures a final consumer product sold at retail or whose brand name is affixed to the consumer product. In the case of a consumer product imported into the United States, manufacturer includes the importer or domestic distributor of the consumer product if the person who manufactured or assembled the consumer product or whose brand name is affixed to the consumer product does not have a presence in the United States.</p>
        <p>(m) "Priority chemical" means a chemical identified by the Department of Health as a chemical of high concern that meets the criteria in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.9403">116.9403</a>.</p>
        <p>(n) "Safer alternative" means an alternative whose potential to harm human health is less than that of the use of a priority chemical that it could replace.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.9401.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/37/#laws.1.47.0">2009 c 37 art 1 s 47</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116.9402">
        <h1 class="shn">116.9402 IDENTIFYING CHEMICALS OF HIGH CONCERN.</h1>
        <p>(a) By July 1, 2010, the department shall, after consultation with the agency, generate a list of chemicals of high concern.</p>
        <p>(b) The department must periodically review and revise the list of chemicals of high concern at least every three years. The department may add chemicals to the list if the chemical meets one or more of the criteria in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.9401">116.9401</a>, paragraph (e).</p>
        <p>(c) The department shall consider chemicals listed as a suspected carcinogen, reproductive or developmental toxicant, or as being persistent, bioaccumulative, and toxic, or very persistent and very bioaccumulative by a state, federal, or international agency. These agencies may include, but are not limited to, the California Environmental Protection Agency, the Washington Department of Ecology, the United States Department of Health, the United States Environmental Protection Agency, the United Nation's World Health Organization, and European Parliament Annex XIV concerning the Registration, Evaluation, Authorisation, and Restriction of Chemicals.</p>
        <p>(d) The department may consider chemicals listed by another state as harmful to human health or the environment for possible inclusion in the list of chemicals of high concern.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.9402.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/37/#laws.1.48.0">2009 c 37 art 1 s 48</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116.9403">
        <h1 class="shn">116.9403 IDENTIFYING PRIORITY CHEMICALS.</h1>
        <p>(a) The department, after consultation with the agency, may designate a chemical of high concern as a priority chemical if the department finds that the chemical:</p>
        <p>(1) has been identified as a high-production volume chemical by the United States Environmental Protection Agency; and</p>
        <p>(2) meets any of the following criteria:</p>
        <p>(i) the chemical has been found through biomonitoring to be present in human blood, including umbilical cord blood, breast milk, urine, or other bodily tissues or fluids;</p>
        <p>(ii) the chemical has been found through sampling and analysis to be present in household dust, indoor air, drinking water, or elsewhere in the home environment; or</p>
        <p>(iii) the chemical has been found through monitoring to be present in fish, wildlife, or the natural environment.</p>
        <p>(b) By February 1, 2011, the department shall publish a list of priority chemicals in the State Register and on the department's Internet website and shall update the published list whenever a new priority chemical is designated.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.9403.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/37/#laws.1.49.0">2009 c 37 art 1 s 49</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116.9405">
        <h1 class="shn">116.9405 APPLICABILITY.</h1>
        <p>The requirements of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.9401">116.9401</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.9407">116.9407</a> do not apply to:</p>
        <p>(1) chemicals in used children's products;</p>
        <p>(2) priority chemicals used in the manufacturing process, but that are not present in the final product;</p>
        <p>(3) priority chemicals used in agricultural production;</p>
        <p>(4) motor vehicles as defined in chapter 168 or watercraft as defined in chapter 86B or their component parts, except that the use of priority chemicals in detachable car seats is not exempt;</p>
        <p>(5) priority chemicals generated solely as combustion by-products or that are present in combustible fuels;</p>
        <p>(6) retailers;</p>
        <p>(7) pharmaceutical products or biologics;</p>
        <p>(8) a medical device as defined in the federal Food, Drug, and Cosmetic Act, United States Code, title 21, section 321(h);</p>
        <p>(9) food and food or beverage packaging, except a container containing baby food or infant formula;</p>
        <p>(10) consumer electronics products and electronic components, including but not limited to personal computers; audio and video equipment; calculators; digital displays; wireless phones; cameras; game consoles; printers; and handheld electronic and electrical devices used to access interactive software or their associated peripherals; or products that comply with the provisions of directive 2002/95/EC of the European Union, adopted by the European Parliament and Council of the European Union now or hereafter in effect; or</p>
        <p class="in">(11) outdoor sport equipment, including snowmobiles as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.81">84.81</a>, subdivision 3; all-terrain vehicles as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.92#stat.84.92.8">84.92, subdivision 8</a>; personal watercraft as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/86B.005#stat.86B.005.14a">86B.005, subdivision 14a</a>; watercraft as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/86B.005#stat.86B.005.18">86B.005, subdivision 18</a>; and off-highway motorcycles, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.787">84.787</a>, subdivision 7, and all attachments and repair parts for all of this equipment.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.9405.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/37/#laws.1.50.0">2009 c 37 art 1 s 50</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116.9406">
        <h1 class="shn">116.9406 DONATIONS TO THE STATE.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner may accept donations, grants, and other funds to carry out the purposes of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.9401">116.9401</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.9407">116.9407</a>. All donations, grants, and other funds must be accepted without preconditions regarding the outcomes of the regulatory oversight processes set forth in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.9401">116.9401</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.9407">116.9407</a>.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.9406.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/37/#laws.1.51.0">2009 c 37 art 1 s 51</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116.9407">
        <h1 class="shn">116.9407 PARTICIPATING IN INTERSTATE CHEMICALS CLEARINGHOUSE.</h1>
        <p>The state may cooperate with other states in an interstate chemicals clearinghouse regarding chemicals in consumer products, including the classification of priority chemicals in commerce; organizing and managing available data on chemicals, including information on uses, hazards, risks, and environmental and health concerns; and producing and evaluating information on safer alternatives to specific uses of priority chemicals.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.9407.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/37/#laws.1.52.0">2009 c 37 art 1 s 52</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116.943">
        <h1 class="shn">116.943 PRODUCTS CONTAINING PFAS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.943.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) For purposes of this section, the following terms have the meanings given.</p>
          <p>(b) "Adult mattress" means a mattress other than a crib mattress or toddler mattress.</p>
          <p>(c) "Air care product" means a chemically formulated consumer product labeled to indicate that the purpose of the product is to enhance or condition the indoor environment by eliminating odors or freshening the air.</p>
          <p>(d) "Automotive maintenance product" means a chemically formulated consumer product labeled to indicate that the purpose of the product is to maintain the appearance of a motor vehicle, including products for washing, waxing, polishing, cleaning, or treating the exterior or interior surfaces of motor vehicles. Automotive maintenance product does not include automotive paint or paint repair products.</p>
          <p>(e) "Carpet or rug" means a fabric marketed or intended for use as a floor covering.</p>
          <p>(f) "Cleaning product" means a finished product used primarily for domestic, commercial, or institutional cleaning purposes, including but not limited to an air care product, an automotive maintenance product, a general cleaning product, or a polish or floor maintenance product.</p>
          <p>(g) "Commissioner" means the commissioner of the Pollution Control Agency.</p>
          <p>(h) "Cookware" means durable houseware items used to prepare, dispense, or store food, foodstuffs, or beverages. Cookware includes but is not limited to pots, pans, skillets, grills, baking sheets, baking molds, trays, bowls, and cooking utensils.</p>
          <p>(i) "Cosmetic" means articles, excluding soap:</p>
          <p>(1) intended to be rubbed, poured, sprinkled, or sprayed on, introduced into, or otherwise applied to the human body or any part thereof for the purpose of cleansing, beautifying, promoting attractiveness, or altering the appearance; and</p>
          <p>(2) intended for use as a component of any such article.</p>
          <p>(j) "Currently unavoidable use" means a use of PFAS that the commissioner has determined by rule under this section to be essential for health, safety, or the functioning of society and for which alternatives are not reasonably available.</p>
          <p>(k) "Fabric treatment" means a substance applied to fabric to give the fabric one or more characteristics, including but not limited to stain resistance or water resistance.</p>
          <p>(l) "Intentionally added" means PFAS deliberately added during the manufacture of a product where the continued presence of PFAS is desired in the final product or one of the product's components to perform a specific function.</p>
          <p>(m) "Juvenile product" means a product designed or marketed for use by infants and children under 12 years of age:</p>
          <p>(1) including but not limited to a baby or toddler foam pillow; bassinet; bedside sleeper; booster seat; changing pad; child restraint system for use in motor vehicles and aircraft; co-sleeper; crib mattress; highchair; highchair pad; infant bouncer; infant carrier; infant seat; infant sleep positioner; infant swing; infant travel bed; infant walker; nap cot; nursing pad; nursing pillow; play mat; playpen; play yard; polyurethane foam mat, pad, or pillow; portable foam nap mat; portable infant sleeper; portable hook-on chair; soft-sided portable crib; stroller; and toddler mattress; and</p>
          <p>(2) not including a children's electronic product such as a personal computer, audio and video equipment, calculator, wireless phone, game console, handheld device incorporating a video screen, or any associated peripheral such as a mouse, keyboard, power supply unit, or power cord; or an adult mattress.</p>
          <p>(n) "Manufacturer" means the person that creates or produces a product or whose brand name is affixed to the product. In the case of a product imported into the United States, manufacturer includes the importer or first domestic distributor of the product if the person that manufactured or assembled the product or whose brand name is affixed to the product does not have a presence in the United States.</p>
          <p>(o) "Medical device" has the meaning given "device" under United States Code, title 21, section 321, subsection (h).</p>
          <p>(p) "Perfluoroalkyl and polyfluoroalkyl substances" or "PFAS" means a class of fluorinated organic chemicals containing at least one fully fluorinated carbon atom.</p>
          <p>(q) "Product" means an item manufactured, assembled, packaged, or otherwise prepared for sale to consumers, including but not limited to its product components, sold or distributed for personal, residential, commercial, or industrial use, including for use in making other products.</p>
          <p>(r) "Product component" means an identifiable component of a product, regardless of whether the manufacturer of the product is the manufacturer of the component.</p>
          <p>(s) "Ski wax" means a lubricant applied to the bottom of snow runners, including but not limited to skis and snowboards, to improve their grip or glide properties. Ski wax includes related tuning products.</p>
          <p>(t) "Textile" means an item made in whole or part from a natural or synthetic fiber, yarn, or fabric. Textile includes but is not limited to leather, cotton, silk, jute, hemp, wool, viscose, nylon, and polyester.</p>
          <p>(u) "Textile furnishings" means textile goods of a type customarily used in households and businesses, including but not limited to draperies, floor coverings, furnishings, bedding, towels, and tablecloths.</p>
          <p>(v) "Upholstered furniture" means an article of furniture that is designed to be used for sitting, resting, or reclining and that is wholly or partly stuffed or filled with any filling material.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.943.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Information required.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) On or before January 1, 2026, a manufacturer of a product sold, offered for sale, or distributed in the state that contains intentionally added PFAS must submit to the commissioner information that includes:</p>
          <p>(1) a brief description of the product, including a universal product code (UPC), stock keeping unit (SKU), or other numeric code assigned to the product;</p>
          <p>(2) the purpose for which PFAS are used in the product, including in any product components;</p>
          <p>(3) the amount of each PFAS, identified by its chemical abstracts service registry number, in the product, reported as an exact quantity determined using commercially available analytical methods or as falling within a range approved for reporting purposes by the commissioner;</p>
          <p>(4) the name and address of the manufacturer and the name, address, and phone number of a contact person for the manufacturer; and</p>
          <p>(5) any additional information requested by the commissioner as necessary to implement the requirements of this section.</p>
          <p>(b) With the approval of the commissioner, a manufacturer may supply the information required in paragraph (a) for a category or type of product rather than for each individual product.</p>
          <p>(c) A manufacturer must submit the information required under this subdivision whenever a new product that contains intentionally added PFAS is sold, offered for sale, or distributed in the state and update and revise the information whenever there is significant change in the information or when requested to do so by the commissioner.</p>
          <p>(d) A person may not sell, offer for sale, or distribute for sale in the state a product containing intentionally added PFAS if the manufacturer has failed to provide the information required under this subdivision and the person has received notification under subdivision 4.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.943.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Information requirement waivers; extensions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner may waive all or part of the information requirement under subdivision 2 if the commissioner determines that substantially equivalent information is already publicly available. The commissioner may grant a waiver under this paragraph to a manufacturer or a group of manufacturers for multiple products or a product category.</p>
          <p>(b) For a pesticide regulated under chapter 18B, a fertilizer, an agricultural liming material, a plant amendment, or a soil amendment regulated under chapter 18C, a manufacturer may satisfy the requirements of subdivision 2 by submitting the information required by that subdivision as part of its annual registration or approval process under chapter 18B or 18C. For information that is regulated under chapters 18B and 18C, the commissioner and the commissioner of agriculture must jointly determine whether to make the information publicly available based on applicable statutes.</p>
          <p>(c) The commissioner may enter into an agreement with one or more other states or political subdivisions of a state to collect information and may accept information to a shared system as meeting the information requirement under subdivision 2.</p>
          <p>(d) The commissioner may extend the deadline for submission by a manufacturer of the information required under subdivision 2 if the commissioner determines that more time is needed by the manufacturer to comply with the submission requirement.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.943.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Testing required and certificate of compliance.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) If the commissioner has reason to believe that a product contains intentionally added PFAS and the product is being offered for sale in the state, the commissioner may direct the manufacturer of the product to, within 30 days, provide the commissioner with testing results that demonstrate the amount of each of the PFAS, identified by its chemical abstracts service registry number, in the product, reported as an exact quantity determined using commercially available analytical methods or as falling within a range approved for reporting purposes by the commissioner.</p>
          <p>(b) If testing demonstrates that the product does not contain intentionally added PFAS, the manufacturer must provide the commissioner a certificate attesting that the product does not contain intentionally added PFAS, including testing results and any other relevant information.</p>
          <p>(c) If testing demonstrates that the product contains intentionally added PFAS, the manufacturer must provide the commissioner with the testing results and the information required under subdivision 2.</p>
          <p>(d) A manufacturer must notify persons who sell or offer for sale a product prohibited under subdivision 2 or 5 that the sale of that product is prohibited in this state and provide the commissioner with a list of the names and addresses of those notified.</p>
          <p>(e) The commissioner may notify persons who sell or offer for sale a product prohibited under subdivision 2 or 5 that the sale of that product is prohibited in this state.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.943.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Prohibitions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Beginning January 1, 2025, a person may not sell, offer for sale, or distribute for sale in this state the following products if the product contains intentionally added PFAS:</p>
          <p>(1) carpets or rugs;</p>
          <p>(2) cleaning products;</p>
          <p>(3) cookware;</p>
          <p>(4) cosmetics;</p>
          <p>(5) dental floss;</p>
          <p>(6) fabric treatments;</p>
          <p>(7) juvenile products;</p>
          <p>(8) menstruation products;</p>
          <p>(9) textile furnishings;</p>
          <p>(10) ski wax; or</p>
          <p>(11) upholstered furniture.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner may by rule identify additional products by category or use that may not be sold, offered for sale, or distributed for sale in this state if they contain intentionally added PFAS and designate effective dates. A prohibition adopted under this paragraph must be effective no earlier than January 1, 2025, and no later than January 1, 2032. The commissioner must prioritize the prohibition of the sale of product categories that, in the commissioner's judgment, are most likely to contaminate or harm the state's environment and natural resources if they contain intentionally added PFAS.</p>
          <p>(c) Beginning January 1, 2032, a person may not sell, offer for sale, or distribute for sale in this state any product that contains intentionally added PFAS, unless the commissioner has determined by rule that the use of PFAS in the product is a currently unavoidable use. The commissioner may specify specific products or product categories for which the commissioner has determined the use of PFAS is a currently unavoidable use. The commissioner may not determine that the use of PFAS in a product is a currently unavoidable use if the product is listed in paragraph (a).</p>
          <p>(d) The commissioner may not take action under paragraph (b) or (c) with respect to a pesticide, as defined under chapter 18B, a fertilizer, an agricultural liming material, a plant amendment, or a soil amendment as defined under chapter 18C, unless the commissioner of agriculture approves the action.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.943.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Fees.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may establish by rule a fee payable by a manufacturer to the commissioner upon submission of the information required under subdivision 2 to cover the agency's reasonable costs to implement this section. Fees collected under this subdivision must be deposited in an account in the environmental fund.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.943.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Enforcement.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner may enforce this section under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115.071">115.071</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.072">116.072</a>. The commissioner may coordinate with the commissioners of agriculture, commerce, and health in enforcing this section.</p>
          <p>(b) When requested by the commissioner, a person must furnish to the commissioner any information that the person may have or may reasonably obtain that is relevant to show compliance with this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.943.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Exemptions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) This section does not apply to:</p>
          <p>(1) a product for which federal law governs the presence of PFAS in the product in a manner that preempts state authority;</p>
          <p>(2) a product regulated under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/325F.072">325F.072</a> or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/325F.075">325F.075</a>; or</p>
          <p>(3) the sale or resale of a used product.</p>
          <p>(b) Subdivisions 4 and 5 do not apply to a prosthetic or orthotic device or to any product that is a medical device or drug or that is otherwise used in a medical setting or in medical applications regulated by the United States Food and Drug Administration.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.943.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Rules.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may adopt rules necessary to implement this section. Section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.125">14.125</a> does not apply to the commissioner's rulemaking authority under this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.943.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Short title.</span></h2>
          <p>This section is "Amara's Law."</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.943.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/60/#laws.3.21.0">2023 c 60 art 3 s 21</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">SMALL BUSINESS ASSISTANCE</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116.95">
        <h1 class="shn">116.95 CITATION.</h1>
        <p>Sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.96">116.96</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.99">116.99</a> may be cited as the "Small Business Air-Quality Compliance Assistance Act."</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.95.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/546/#laws.0.5.0">1992 c 546 s 5</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116.96">
        <h1 class="shn">116.96 DEFINITIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.96.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Scope.</span></h2>
          <p>The definitions in this section apply to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.96">116.96</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.99">116.99</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.96.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Agency.</span></h2>
          <p>"Agency" means the Pollution Control Agency.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.96.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Clean Air Act.</span></h2>
          <p>"Clean Air Act" means the federal Clean Air Act, United States Code, title 42, section 7401 et seq., as amended.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.96.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Commissioner.</span></h2>
          <p>"Commissioner" means the commissioner of the Pollution Control Agency.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.96.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Regulated pollutant.</span></h2>
          <p>"Regulated pollutant" means:</p>
          <p>(1) a volatile organic compound that participates in atmospheric photochemical reactions;</p>
          <p>(2) a pollutant for which a national ambient air quality standard has been promulgated;</p>
          <p>(3) a pollutant that is addressed by a standard promulgated under section 7411 or 7412 of the Clean Air Act; or</p>
          <p>(4) any pollutant that is regulated under this chapter or air quality rules adopted under this chapter.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.96.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Small business stationary source.</span></h2>
          <p>"Small business stationary source" means a business that:</p>
          <p>(1) is owned or operated by a person that employs 100 or fewer individuals;</p>
          <p>(2) is a small business concern as defined in the Small Business Act, United States Code, title 15, section 632(a);</p>
          <p>(3) is not a major stationary source as defined in section 7661 of the Clean Air Act;</p>
          <p>(4) does not emit 50 tons or more per year of any regulated pollutant; and</p>
          <p>(5) emits less than 75 tons per year of all regulated pollutants.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.96.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/546/#laws.0.6.0">1992 c 546 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/220/#laws.0.107.0">1995 c 220 s 107</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116.97">
        <h1 class="shn">116.97 SMALL BUSINESS AIR-QUALITY COMPLIANCE ASSISTANCE PROGRAM.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.97.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Creation.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall establish a small business air-quality compliance assistance program that incorporates the small business stationary source technical and environmental compliance assistance program required by section 7661f of the Clean Air Act.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.97.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Requirements.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall ensure that the program provides at least the following:</p>
          <p>(1) direct, timely, one-on-one information and technical assistance to small businesses that are stationary sources on matters including, but not limited to, their legal rights and obligations under federal and state air quality laws and regulations, applicable requirements and alternatives for achieving compliance, permit procedures, preparation of permit applications, sources of technical expertise, consequences of operating in violation, enforcement, fines, penalties, and appeals;</p>
          <p>(2) a clearinghouse to provide information and referral to appropriate technical experts concerning Clean Air Act regulatory requirements, compliance methods, and control technologies;</p>
          <p>(3) information and assistance on methods of pollution prevention and the prevention and detection of accidental releases;</p>
          <p>(4) audits of the operations of small business stationary sources to determine compliance with federal and state air quality laws and regulations, or establishment of a procedure for referring sources to qualified auditors. Audits may include, but need not be limited to, an evaluation of work practices, compliance monitoring procedures, record-keeping requirements, and technical assistance on pollution prevention opportunities and control options;</p>
          <p>(5) to the extent permitted by federal and state air quality laws and regulations, procedures for responding to requests from small business stationary sources for modification of work practices or methods compliance because of the financial or technological capability of the source; and</p>
          <p>(6) coordination of efforts with trade associations, small business assistance providers, and federal, state, and local governmental agencies that provide information and technical assistance to small businesses, in order to maximize the information and assistance available to small businesses and to prevent duplication of effort and services.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.97.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/546/#laws.0.7.0">1992 c 546 s 7</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116.98">
        <h1 class="shn">116.98 OMBUDSMAN FOR SMALL BUSINESS AIR-QUALITY COMPLIANCE ASSISTANCE PROGRAM.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.98.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Appointment.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall appoint an ombudsman for small business air-quality compliance assistance in the classified service.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.98.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Duties.</span></h2>
          <p>The ombudsman shall provide direct oversight of the small business air-quality compliance assistance program. The ombudsman's duties include, but are not limited to:</p>
          <p>(1) conducting independent evaluations of all aspects of the program;</p>
          <p>(2) monitoring, reviewing, and providing comments and recommendations to federal, state, and local air quality authorities on laws and regulations that impact small businesses;</p>
          <p>(3) facilitating and promoting the participation of small businesses in the development of laws and regulations that affect them;</p>
          <p>(4) providing reports to federal, state, and local air quality authorities and the public on the requirements of the Clean Air Act and their impact on small businesses;</p>
          <p>(5) disseminating information concerning proposed air quality regulations, control technologies, and other information to small businesses and other interested parties;</p>
          <p>(6) participating in and sponsoring meetings and conferences concerning air quality laws and regulations with state and local regulatory officials, industry groups, and small business representatives;</p>
          <p>(7) investigating and assisting in the resolution of complaints and disputes from small businesses against state or local air quality authorities;</p>
          <p>(8) periodically reviewing the work and services provided by the program with trade associations and small business representatives;</p>
          <p>(9) operating a toll-free telephone line to provide free, confidential help on individual source problems and grievances;</p>
          <p>(10) referring small businesses to appropriate technical specialists for information and assistance on affordable alternative technologies, process changes, products, and operational methods to help reduce air pollution and accidental releases;</p>
          <p>(11) arranging for and assisting in the preparation of program guideline documents to ensure that the language is readily understandable by the lay person;</p>
          <p>(12) establishing cooperative programs with trade associations and small businesses to promote and achieve voluntary compliance with federal and state air quality laws and regulations;</p>
          <p>(13) establishing cooperative programs with federal, state, and local governmental entities and the private sector to assist small businesses in securing sources of funds to comply with federal, state, and local air quality laws and regulations;</p>
          <p>(14) conducting studies to evaluate the impacts of federal and state air quality laws and regulations on the state's economy, local economies, and small businesses;</p>
          <p>(15) serving as a voting member of the Small Business Air-Quality Compliance Advisory Council established by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.99">116.99</a>; and</p>
          <p>(16) performing the ombudsman's duties in cooperation and coordination with governmental entities and private organizations as appropriate so as to eliminate overlap and duplication to the extent practicable.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.98.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Independence of action.</span></h2>
          <p>In carrying out the duties imposed by sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.96">116.96</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.99">116.99</a>, the ombudsman may act independently of the agency in providing testimony to the legislature, contacting and making periodic reports to federal and state officials as necessary to carry out the duties imposed by sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.96">116.96</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.99">116.99</a>, and addressing problems of concern to small businesses.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.98.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Qualifications.</span></h2>
          <p>The ombudsman must be knowledgeable about federal and state air quality laws and regulations, control technologies, and federal and state legislative and regulatory processes. The ombudsman must be experienced in dealing with both private enterprise and governmental entities, arbitration and negotiation, interpretation of laws and regulations, investigation, record keeping, report writing, public speaking, and management.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.98.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Office support.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall provide the ombudsman with the necessary office space, supplies, equipment, and clerical support to effectively perform the duties imposed by sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.96">116.96</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.99">116.99</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.98.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/546/#laws.0.8.0">1992 c 546 s 8</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116.99">
        <h1 class="shn">116.99 SMALL BUSINESS AIR-QUALITY COMPLIANCE ADVISORY COUNCIL.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.99.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Creation.</span></h2>
          <p>A Small Business Air-Quality Compliance Assistance Advisory Council is established within the agency.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.99.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Duties.</span></h2>
          <p>The council has the following duties:</p>
          <p>(1) rendering advisory opinions on the effectiveness of the program, difficulties encountered, and degree and severity of enforcement;</p>
          <p>(2) preparing periodic reports on matters relating to the program as requested by appropriate federal and state agencies;</p>
          <p>(3) reviewing information for sources to ensure the information is complete, comprehensive, and understandable to the lay person; and</p>
          <p>(4) other duties it finds appropriate to comply with applicable federal or state air quality laws and regulations.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.99.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Membership.</span></h2>
          <p>The council consists of the following members:</p>
          <p>(1) two members appointed by the governor who represent the general public and are not owners or representatives of owners who are small business stationary sources;</p>
          <p>(2) the commissioner or the commissioner's designee, who shall represent the agency;</p>
          <p>(3) four members appointed by the legislature who are owners or representatives of owners of small business stationary sources; and</p>
          <p>(4) the commissioner of employment and economic development or the commissioner's designee.</p>
          <p>The majority and minority leaders of the house of representatives and the senate shall each appoint one of the members listed in clause (3).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.99.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Membership terms; compensation; removal.</span></h2>
          <p>The membership terms, compensation, and removal of council members are governed by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/15.0575">15.0575</a>, except that subdivision 5 does not apply.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.99.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Chair.</span></h2>
          <p>The council shall select its chair by a majority vote.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.99.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Program.</span></h2>
          <p>The council may set its own agenda and work program, consistent with the requirements of the Clean Air Act, after consultation with the commissioner and the small business ombudsman established by this chapter.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.99.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Funding.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall allocate and administer the funds reasonably necessary to cover the operational costs of the council.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.99.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Staff.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall provide staff services reasonably required by the council.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.99.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/546/#laws.0.9.0">1992 c 546 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/247/#laws.2.54.0">1995 c 247 art 2 s 54</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/4/#laws.0.1.0">1Sp2003 c 4 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.2.161.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 2 s 161</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116.991"><b>116.991</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/216/#laws.0.160.0">1997 c 216 s 160</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.116.992"><b>116.992</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/216/#laws.0.160.0">1997 c 216 s 160</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116.993">
        <h1 class="shn">116.993 SMALL BUSINESS ENVIRONMENTAL-IMPROVEMENT LOAN PROGRAM.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.993.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Establishment.</span></h2>
          <p>A small business environmental-improvement revolving loan program is established to provide loans to small businesses for the purpose of capital equipment purchases that will meet or exceed environmental rules and regulations or for investigation and cleanup of contaminated sites. The small business environmental-improvement revolving loan program replaces the small business environmental loan program in Minnesota Statutes 1996, section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.991">116.991</a>, and the hazardous waste generator loan program in Minnesota Statutes 1996, section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.223">115B.223</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.993.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Eligible borrower.</span></h2>
          <p>To be eligible for a loan under this section, a borrower must:</p>
          <p>(1) be a small business corporation, sole proprietorship, partnership, or association;</p>
          <p>(2) be a potential emitter of pollutants to the air, ground, or water;</p>
          <p>(3) need capital for equipment purchases that will meet or exceed environmental regulations or need capital for site investigation and cleanup;</p>
          <p>(4) have less than 100 full-time equivalent employees; and</p>
          <p>(5) have an after tax profit of less than $500,000.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.993.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Loan application and award procedure.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of the Pollution Control Agency may give priority to applicants that include, but are not limited to, those subject to Clean Air Act standards adopted under United States Code, title 42, section 7412, those undergoing site investigation and remediation, those involved with facility wide environmental compliance and pollution prevention projects, and those determined by the commissioner to be small business outreach priorities. The commissioner shall decide whether to award a loan to an eligible borrower based on:</p>
          <p>(1) the applicant's financial need;</p>
          <p>(2) the applicant's ability to secure and repay the loan; and</p>
          <p>(3) the expected environmental benefit.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.993.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Screening committee.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall appoint a screening committee to evaluate applications and determine loan awards. The committee shall have diverse expertise in air quality, water quality, solid and hazardous waste management, site response and cleanup, pollution prevention, and financial analysis.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.993.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Limit on availability.</span></h2>
          <p>Numbers of applications accepted, evaluated, and awarded are based upon the available money in the small business environmental-improvement loan account.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.116.993.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Loan conditions.</span></h2>
          <p>A loan made under this section must include:</p>
          <p>(1) an interest rate that is at or below one-half the prime rate, not to exceed five percent;</p>
          <p>(2) a term of payment of not more than seven years; and</p>
          <p>(3) an amount not less than $1,000 or exceeding $75,000.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.993.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/216/#laws.0.117.0">1997 c 216 s 117</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/4/#laws.3.103.0">1Sp2019 c 4 art 3 s 103</a>,104</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.116.994">
        <h1 class="shn">116.994 SMALL BUSINESS ENVIRONMENTAL-IMPROVEMENT LOAN ACCOUNTING.</h1>
        <p>Repayments of loans made under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.993">116.993</a> must be credited to the environmental fund. Money deposited in the fund under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.993">116.993</a> is appropriated to the commissioner for loans under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.993">116.993</a>.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.116.994.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/216/#laws.0.118.0">1997 c 216 s 118</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/128/#laws.2.40.0">2003 c 128 art 2 s 40</a></p>
      </div>
    </div>
